\providecommand{\PhiFA}{\Phi^{\mathrm{FA}}}
\providecommand{\HolFA}{\mathrm{HolFA}}
\providecommand{\EdHolFA}{E_d\text{-}\HolFA}
\providecommand{\EnHolFA}{E_n\text{-}\HolFA}
\providecommand{\SpCh}{\mathrm{Sp}^{\mathrm{ch}}}
\providecommand{\intSigma}{\textstyle\int_{\Sigma_{d-1}}}
\providecommand{\hCS}{\mathrm{hCS}}
\providecommand{\kchBV}{\kappa_{\mathrm{ch,BV}}}
\providecommand{\kfiber}{\kappa_{\mathrm{fiber}}}

\chapter{The \texorpdfstring{$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$}{kappa-ch} Stratification by CY Dimension}
\label{ch:cy-d-kappa-stratification}

The two-stage factorisation functor
\[
 \Phi_d \;=\; \SpCh_{\Sigma_{d-1}, C} \circ \PhiFA_d
 \colon\; \mathrm{CY}_d\text{-}\mathrm{Cat}
 \;\longrightarrow\; \EdHolFA(X)
 \;\longrightarrow\; \EnHolFA(C)
\]
(see \cite{VolI},
Definitions~\ref{def:phi-fa-and-sp}--\ref{rem:multiple-e1-specialisations})
assigns to a CY$_d$ category $\cC$ first a holomorphic factorisation
algebra $\PhiFA_d(\cC)$ on the CY$_d$ target $X$, then a chiral algebra
\(\SpCh_{\Sigma_{d-1},C}(\PhiFA_d(\cC))\) on a curve. In this chapter
the Hodge-supertrace computations are unconditional Hodge theory; their
interpretation as \(\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}\) of a chiral output is proved on
the constructed \(d\leq 3\) loci and is conditional for \(d\geq 4\).
The specialisation is factorisation homology over \(\Sigma_{d-1}\).
Five scalar invariants of distinct
provenance attach to this two-stage datum; four inhabiting the
main text $(\kch, \kcat, \kBKM, \kfiber)$ plus the one-loop
Costello--Li BCOV curving coefficient $\kchBV$ valued on the
compactly-supported side; and no two coincide in general. The
discipline of never conflating them is the unit of analysis of this
chapter.

\begin{definition}[The four \texorpdfstring{$\kappa_\bullet$}{kappa-.}-invariants]
\label{def:four-kappa-bullet-invariants}
For a CY$_d$ manifold $X$ with CY$_d$ category $\cC = D^b(\Coh(X))$,
the following four scalar invariants, jointly denoted
$\kappa_\bullet \in \{\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}},\kappa_{\mathrm{cat}},
\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}},\kappa_{\mathrm{fiber}}\}$, are defined.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
\item \emph{$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\cA_X)$.} The degree-two shadow
 coefficient of the chiral algebra
 $\cA_X = \Phi_d(\cC)$, computed as the Hodge supertrace on compact
 CY$_d$ at even $d$:
 \[
 \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\cA_X) \;=\; \Xi(X)
 \;:=\; \sum_{q = 0}^{d} (-1)^q\, h^{0, q}(X)
 \qquad\text{(Theorem~\ref{thm:kappa-hodge-supertrace-identification}).}
 \]
 Native to the $\PhiFA_d(\cC)$ stage; at odd $d$ read chain-level
 (Remark~\ref{rem:cy-d-kappa-ch-odd-d-chain-level}).
\item \emph{$\kappa_{\mathrm{cat}}(\cC) = \chi(\cO_X)$.} The categorical
 Euler characteristic of the structure sheaf, K\"unneth-multiplicative
 on products: $\kappa_{\mathrm{cat}}(X \times Y) = \kappa_{\mathrm{cat}}(X)
 \cdot \kappa_{\mathrm{cat}}(Y)$. Native to the variety $X$,
 independent of the $\PhiFA_d$ stage.
\item \emph{$\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\Phi) = c(0)/2$.} The weight of a
 Borcherds product $\Phi$, equal to half the constant term $c(0)$ of
 the Borcherds-input principal part
 (Theorem~\ref{thm:borcherds-weight-kappa-BKM-universal}). Attached to
 the $\SpCh$ specialisation cycle, not to the $\PhiFA_d$ output.
\item \emph{$\kappa_{\mathrm{fiber}}$.} The rank or Euler characteristic
 of the fibering data attached to the $\SpCh$ map; for a K3 fibration
 it is the Mukai-lattice rank $24$ of the fibre.
\item \emph{$\kchBV(\cA_X)$.} The Costello--Li one-loop BCOV curving
 coefficient at $\beta\gamma$-ghost central charge $c = -2$, valued
 on compact $X$ in $H^1(X, \cO_X)$ against the $\beta\gamma$-ghost
 supertrace sign and on non-compact $X$ in $H^1_c(X, \cO_X)$ (or
 equivalently, via Alexander duality, in the boundary trace on the
 link $\partial X$). Concretely, the Atiyah--BCOV dilaton cocycle
 is the class
 \[
 \alpha_{\mathrm{BCOV}}
 \;=\; \frac{\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(X)}{24} \cdot
 \mathrm{tr}\, \mathrm{At}(T_X)
 \;\in\; H^1(X, \cO_X),
 \]
 where $\mathrm{At}(T_X) \in \Ext^1(T_X, T_X \otimes \Omega^1_X)$ is
 the Atiyah class and $\mathrm{tr}$ is the trace pairing
 $\Omega^1_X \otimes T_X \to \cO_X$; for strict CY, although
 $c_1(T_X) = 0$ in $H^{1,1}(X)$, the Dolbeault component
 $\mathrm{tr}\,\mathrm{At}(T_X) \in H^{0,1}(X, \cO_X)$ need not
 vanish (the $c_1 = 0$ CY condition lives in $H^{1,1}$, not in
 $H^{0,1}$; the two are distinct Hodge components of the Chern-class
 data). The class is computed via the Atiyah-class extension of
 the tangent sheaf (Kapranov \texttt{math/9811023}) and pairs
 through the BV one-loop Quantum Master Equation of Costello--Li
 (\texttt{arXiv:1606.00365} Prop.~5.2) with the $\beta\gamma$-ghost
 zero mode $\omega_{\mathrm{ghost}}$ to produce the BCOV dilaton
 free energy of BCOV (\texttt{hep-th/9309140} Eq.~(5.11)). Native to the
 stratum-(ii--iv) BCOV sector on $\PhiFA_d(\cC)$; vanishes on
 stratum-(i) toric cells with zero-dimensional $T$-fixed locus by the
 Atiyah-retraction $X \simeq X^T$ collapsing $\mathrm{At}(T_X)$ to
 the $T$-weight-balanced zero class (Kapranov
 \texttt{math/9811023}). Scope: $\kchBV$ targets $H^1(X, \cO_X)$ on
 compact $X$ and $H^1_c(X, \cO_X)$ on non-compact $X$, \emph{not}
 $H^\bullet(\cC)$; it is a first-principles Atiyah-class-of-$T_X$
 coefficient on the geometric target, not a Hochschild invariant of
 the category.
\end{enumerate}
These are five construction-distinct invariants of a single CY datum
$(\cC, X)$; four are native to the main text ($\kch, \kcat, \kBKM,
\kfiber$) and the fifth $\kchBV$ is activated when the one-loop
BCOV curving is non-trivial; i.e.\ when the dilaton cocycle
$\mathrm{tr}\, \mathrm{At}(T_X)$ survives as a nonzero class in
$H^1(X, \cO_X)$ on compact $X$, or equivalently when the boundary
link $\partial X$ of a non-compact $X$ supports a nontrivial
$\beta\gamma$-ghost zero mode. Any identity relating two or more of
the five is a theorem about the specific construction; no pair
coincides by definition.
\end{definition}

\emph{Odd-$d$ failure via Serre involution.} At odd $d$ the Serre
involution $\sigma \colon q \mapsto d - q$ acts on $h^{0, \bullet}(X)$
with character $(-1)^d = -1$, so Serre duality $h^{0, q} = h^{0, d - q}$
pairs each $q < d/2$ with its sign-opposite partner and the pairs cancel
uniformly: $\Xi(X) = 0$ for every compact CY$_d$ at odd $d$
(Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-d-tri-stratum}, stratum (I)). The Hodge-supertrace
formula $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\cA_X) = \Xi(X)$ is vacuous on this
stratum and must be replaced by direct chain-level computation:
$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\mathrm{loc}\,\mathbb{P}^2) = 3/2$ via the
$\mathbb{Z}/3$-McKay quiver (Theorem~\ref{thm:local-p2-shadow}) and
$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\mathrm{conifold}) = 1$ via direct McKay
(Corollary~\ref{cor:conifold-non-local-surface}) are the two canonical
chain-level readings at $d = 3$ that expose the Serre-silent algebraic
content. Hence: at even $d$, $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ is the Hodge
supertrace; at odd $d$, $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ reads chain-level through
the McKay quiver or the direct shadow computation of the relevant
non-compact toric CY$_3$.

\emph{Canonical $K3 \times E$ five-spectrum.} The Calabi--Yau
threefold $K3 \times E$ carries the canonical five-valued spectrum
\[
 \bigl\{
 \kappa_{\mathrm{cat}},\
 \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Hodge}},\
 \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Heis}},\
 \kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\fg_{\Delta_5}),\
 \kappa_{\mathrm{fiber}}
 \bigr\}(K3 \times E)
 \;=\; \{0,\, 0,\, 3,\, 5,\, 24\}
\]
from five distinct constructions on the same Calabi--Yau datum.
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=1.8em,itemsep=1pt]
\item $\kappa_{\mathrm{cat}}(K3 \times E) = \chi(\cO_{K3}) \cdot
\chi(\cO_E) = 2 \cdot 0 = 0$ by K\"unneth multiplicativity for
coherent cohomology (Hartshorne, Chapter~III, Proposition~$8.5$).
\item $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Hodge}}(K3 \times E) =
\Xi(K3 \times E) = 0$ via
$\Xi(K3 \times E) = \Xi(K3) \cdot \Xi(E) = 2 \cdot 0$ on the
Hodge supertrace, with $\Xi(E) = 1 - 1 = 0$ killing the product.
\item $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Heis}}(K3 \times E) = 3$
from the Heisenberg-Mukai specialisation, with the Mukai-enhanced
Hodge supertrace giving
$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Heis}}(K3) = 2$ on the K3 fibre and
$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Heis}}(E) = 1$ on the elliptic
factor (Remark~\ref{rem:four-kappa-stage-assignment-cy-d-strat}).
\item $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\fg_{\Delta_5}) = c_1(0)/2 = 5$ from
the Borcherds weight of Gritsenko's paramodular cusp form
$\Delta_5$
(Theorem~\ref{thm:borcherds-weight-kappa-BKM-universal}).
\item $\kappa_{\mathrm{fiber}}(K3) = \mathrm{rank}(\widetilde H(K3,
\Z)) = 24$ the Mukai-lattice rank of the K3 fibre.
\end{itemize}
The five values inhabit five distinct cohomology theories on a
single Calabi--Yau threefold; no two coincide by definition. The
two zeros $\kappa_{\mathrm{cat}} = 0$ and
$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Hodge}} = 0$ vanish for distinct
reasons: K\"unneth on the structure-sheaf Euler for the first,
Serre cancellation on the elliptic factor for the second.
The value $2$ is the K3 fibre $\kappa_{\mathrm{cat}}(K3) =
\chi(\cO_{K3}) = 1 + 1$ and not a total-space entry; misreading
the fibre as the total space is the confusion the five-invariant
discipline forestalls.

\emph{Universal Borcherds weight at two scopes.} The identity
$\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\Phi) = c(0)/2$ holds in two canonical
parametrisations of the paramodular simplest-divisor family.
\emph{Scope~(A): CHL-averaged paramodular ladder.} On the five-entry
CHL ladder $N \in \{1, 2, 3, 4, 6\}$; the orders with an elliptic
curve automorphism of matching order fixing the origin; the
Gritsenko--Nikulin $1995$ Pt II Theorem~$2.1$ construction (additive
lift of the $g_N$-twined weight-$0$ weak Jacobi form
$\phi_{0, 1}^{(g_N)}$) yields paramodular cusp forms
$\Delta_{k(N)}^{(N)}$ of weights
$\mathrm{wt}(\Delta_{k(N)}^{(N)}) = k(N) = (5, 4, 3, 2, 1)$ with
$(c_N(0)) = (10, 8, 6, 4, 2)$; this is the convention deployed in
Theorem~\ref{thm:borcherds-weight-kappa-BKM-universal} below. Scope~(A)
extends to the Mukai-admissible orders $N \in \{5, 7, 8\}$ via
Gritsenko $1994$ Proposition~$3.1$ with $\SpFour$-paramodular weight
$(2, 1, 1)$ (Corollary~\ref{cor:borcherds-weight-full-8form-scope});
at \(N\in\{5,7,8\}\) the missing datum is a smooth \(K3\times E\) CHL
quotient of matching order.
\emph{Scope~(B): Gritsenko--Cl\'ery $2008$ Theorem~$1.4$ classification.}
The eight dd-modular forms with divisor equal to the diagonal
$\mathcal{H}_1 \subset \mathbb{H}_2$ of vanishing order $1$ on
$\Gamma_t(N)$, indexed by triples $(t, N; k) \in \bigl\{(1, 1; 5),
(2, 1; 2), (1, 2; 3), (3, 1; 1), (1, 3; 2), (4, 1; \tfrac{1}{2}),
(1, 4; \tfrac{3}{2}), (2, 2; 1)\bigr\}$, each satisfying
$\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(F_{t, N}) = \mathrm{wt}(F_{t, N}) = k_{t, N}$
by the cusp-weighted Borcherds automorphic product formula of
Gritsenko--Cl\'ery $2008$ Theorem~$3.1$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:eight-form-cy-host-catalogue}).
Six integer-weight entries live on $\Gamma_t(N)$-paramodular
subgroups; two half-integer-weight entries carry multiplier systems of
orders $8$ and $4$ respectively, using the Shimura $1975$ holomorphic-
square-root convention, not a metaplectic cover of $\SpFour$. The two
scopes coincide at $(t, N) = (1, 1)$ (the common entry
$F_{1, 1} = \Delta_5$, weight $5$) and otherwise enumerate distinct
Borcherds products on distinct paramodular groups. The twice-weight tuple
of the GC catalogue is $(10,4,6,2,4,1,3,2)$, not the CHL constant table
$(10,8,6,4,2)$. Scalar-square identities therefore have to be read
row-by-row with their own levels, covers, branches, and denominator data;
they do not identify the CHL $N \in \{2,3,4\}$ ladder with the GC
$(1,N)$ rows.

\emph{Refutation of the additive identity.} The additive identity
\[
 \kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\Phi_N) \;=\;
 \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\cA_{X_N}) + \chi(\cO_{\mathrm{fiber}_N})
\]
fails at every $N \in \{1, 2, 3, 4, 6\}$: at $N = 1$, LHS $= 5$ while
RHS $= 0 + 0 = 0$; at $N = 2$, LHS $= 4$ while RHS $= 1 + 0 = 1$. The
apparent decomposition $5 = 2 + 3$ at $N = 1$ (K3 fibre $\chi(\cO_{K3})
= 2$ plus Heisenberg-Mukai $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Heis}} = 3$)
is a coincidence of two distinct Stage-$2$ constructions, not a
structural identity; the universal formula is
$\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\Phi_N) = c_N(0)/2$ throughout, with
$\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}$ read off the Borcherds weight of the paramodular
form, not summed from supertraces on the product.

\emph{Root cause at odd $d$.} The invariant $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ is
additive under direct sums but not under fibre products: for
$X = Y \times_Z W$ of CY dimensions $(\dim Y, \dim W, \dim Z)$, the
Hodge supertrace $\Xi(X)$ is not determined by $\Xi(Y), \Xi(W), \Xi(Z)$
alone at odd fibre-product dimension. The same fact forces the
chain-level reading at odd $d$: on compact CY$_d$ with $d$ odd, Serre
cancellation collapses every $\Phi$-output Hodge column uniformly to
$\Xi = 0$ regardless of the algebraic richness of $\cA_X$, and the
true invariant is the chain-level McKay shadow of the non-compact
toric model or the direct Hochschild computation on the quiver
(Remark~\ref{rem:cy-d-kappa-ch-odd-d-chain-level}).

\emph{Canonical conifold row and the Polyakov ghost-mode balance.}
On the non-compact CY$_3$ frontier, the resolved conifold
$X_{\mathrm{con}} = \mathrm{Tot}(\cO(-1)^{\oplus 2} \to \P^1)$
carries the canonical four-subscript row
\[
 (\kch, \kcat, \kappa_{\mathrm{vertex}}, \kchBV)(X_{\mathrm{con}})
 \;=\; (+1,\,0,\,+1,\,-1)
\]
(Theorem~\ref{thm:conifold-polyakov-ghost-balance}). The fifth
invariant $\kchBV$ is the Costello--Li one-loop BCOV curving
coefficient against the $\beta\gamma$-ghost supertrace sign at
$\beta\gamma$-system central charge $c = -2$ \textup{(}Costello--Li
\texttt{arXiv:1606.00365} Prop.~5.2; BCOV \texttt{hep-th/9309140}
Eq.~(5.11); the $c = -2$ $\beta\gamma$-system is the Kausch
\texttt{hep-th/9510149} holomorphic avatar of the
critical-dimension argument\textup{);} on compact $X$ with
$H^1(X, \cO_X) \neq 0$ it is the Atiyah-dilaton pairing
$(\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(X)/24) \cdot \mathrm{tr}\,\mathrm{At}(T_X)
\in H^1(X, \cO_X)$ integrated against the $\beta\gamma$-ghost
zero mode, and on non-compact $X$ whose ambient $H^1(X, \cO_X)$
and $H^1_c(X, \cO_X)$ both vanish (e.g.\ the resolved conifold,
\S\ref{sec:cy-strat-conifold}) it is the
$\zeta$-regularised $\beta\gamma$ Casimir energy on the
Sasaki--Einstein boundary link $\partial X$. The three
apparent conifold values $+1, 0, -1$; the DT count on the
compact curve class, the structure-sheaf Euler on the non-compact
total space, and the Costello--Li one-loop BV curving as Casimir
energy on the Sasaki--Einstein boundary link $T^{1, 1} \cong S^2
\times S^3$ of the conifold (Klebanov--Witten
\texttt{hep-th/9807080}); inhabit three distinct cohomology
theories \textup{(}Kontsevich--Soibelman DT/motivic, ordinary
$H^*(X, \cO_X)$, and $\beta\gamma$-ghost vacuum-energy on
$\partial X$\textup{),} and the Polyakov ghost-mode balance
\[
 \kch(X_{\mathrm{con}}) + \kchBV(X_{\mathrm{con}})
 \;=\; \kcat(X_{\mathrm{con}}),
 \qquad (+1) + (-1) \;=\; 0,
\]
registers critical-dimension ghost cancellation in the
$\beta\gamma$-ghost system at $c = -2$ (Polyakov 1981
\emph{Gauge Fields and Strings} Ch.~9 critical-dimension
paradigm; $c = -2$ avatar via Kausch
\texttt{hep-th/9510149}\textup{)}, instantiated on non-compact
CY$_3$ via Costello--Li holomorphic BV quantisation. The balance
is a $\mathsf G$-class non-compact CY$_3$ theorem, not the
Theorem~C ceiling $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} + \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^!
\in \{0, 8, 13, 250/3, 98/3\}$ and not a universal identity
across strata; see
Remark~\ref{rem:conifold-canonical-row-scope} for the precise
scope.

This chapter evaluates the Hodge supertrace dimension by dimension for
$d \in \{1, 2, 3, 4, 5\}$ and stratifies the comparison between
$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ and $\kappa_{\mathrm{cat}}$ by Beauville--Bogomolov
holonomy type. The companion engine is
\texttt{compute/lib/cy\_d\_kappa\_d3.py} with stratification test
\texttt{compute/tests/test\_cy\_d\_kappa\_stratification.py}.

\begin{remark}[Odd-\texorpdfstring{$d$}{d} \texorpdfstring{$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$}{kappa-ch} reads chain-level]
\label{rem:cy-d-kappa-ch-odd-d-chain-level}
At odd complex dimension $d$ the Hodge-supertrace identification
$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\cA_X) = \Xi(X)$ collapses to $0$ by Serre,
and the substantive algebraic content of $\cA_X$ is read instead
through the chain-level McKay quiver attached to the non-compact
toric CY$_3$ model (or directly through the Hochschild cohomology of
the path algebra). At $d = 3$ the two canonical chain-level readings
are:
\begin{itemize}
\item \emph{Local $\mathbb{P}^2 = \mathrm{Tot}(\omega_{\mathbb{P}^2})$.}
The $\mathbb{Z}/3$-McKay quiver of $\mathbb{C}^3 / \mathbb{Z}_3$
(three arrows, three nodes) gives $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}
(\cA_{\mathrm{loc}\,\mathbb{P}^2}) = \tfrac{1}{2}
\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(\mathbb{P}^2) = 3/2$ via the compactly-supported
Dolbeault supertrace on the base (Theorem~\ref{thm:local-p2-shadow}).
\item \emph{Resolved conifold $\mathrm{Tot}(\cO(-1)^{\oplus 2} \to \P^1)$.}
The Kronecker quiver with two arrows in each direction gives
$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\cA_{\mathrm{conifold}}) = 1$ via direct McKay
(Corollary~\ref{cor:conifold-non-local-surface}).
\end{itemize}
Both values are Serre-silent on the $h^{0, \bullet}$ column --
the local $\mathbb{P}^2$ has $h^{0, \bullet}$ column not well-defined
as a compact CY datum, and the conifold contribution $1$ comes from
the single compact cycle $\P^1$ rather than from a Hodge sum.
\end{remark}

\section{Setup: \texorpdfstring{$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$}{kappa-ch}, \texorpdfstring{$\chi(\cO_X)$}{chi(O-X)}, and the Hodge
supertrace}
\label{sec:cy-strat-setup}

Let $X$ be a compact Calabi--Yau manifold of complex dimension $d$, so
that $\omega_X \simeq \cO_X$ and the Serre functor on $D^b(\Coh(X))$ is
the shift $[d]$. Write $h^{p,q} = h^{p,q}(X) = \dim_\C H^q(X, \Omega^p_X)$
for its Hodge numbers and $\chi_p = \chi_p(X) = \sum_q (-1)^q h^{p,q}$
for its holomorphic Euler characteristics (the $p = 0$ case recovers
$\chi(\cO_X)$).

Fix the CY-to-chiral correspondence $\Phi_d$ and write $\cA_X =
\Phi_d(D^b(\Coh(X)))$ for the chiral algebra associated to $X$. The
\emph{algebraization scalar} $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\cA_X)$ is the
degree-two shadow invariant of Vol~I: the coefficient of $[b \mid b]$
in the quadratic piece of the Maurer--Cartan obstruction on
$B^{\mathrm{ord}}(\cA_X)$. It is an algebraization
invariant in the sense of a second algebraization of the same
topological CY$_d$ manifold can produce a different
$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$. By contrast, $\kappa_{\mathrm{cat}}(X)$,
$\kappa_{\mathrm{fiber}}(X)$ are topological manifold invariants;
$\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}$ depends on the Borcherds frame shape.

\subsection{Three candidate comparators}
\label{subsec:cy-strat-three-candidates}

We compare $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ against three Hodge-derived scalars:

\begin{itemize}
\item The \emph{holomorphic Euler characteristic}
$\chi(\cO_X) = \sum_q (-1)^q h^{0,q}(X) = \chi_0(X)$.
\item The \emph{Hodge-filtered supertrace} of the structure sheaf column
\[
 \Xi(X) \;:=\; \sum_{q = 0}^{d} (-1)^q\, h^{0,q}(X).
\]
Because $h^{0,q}$ counts antiholomorphic forms of pure type $(0,q)$,
$\Xi(X) = \chi(\cO_X)$ as \emph{numbers}; the distinction is conceptual
(we shall see the $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ route produces the supertrace
even when $\chi(\cO_X) = 0$ is forced to zero by Serre). The notation
$\Xi$ underlines the supertrace view and suppresses the
$h^{0,q} = h^{d,d-q}$ identification that flattens it back to
$\chi(\cO_X)$.
\item The \emph{explicit algebraic Hochschild sum}
$\mathrm{HH}_{\mathrm{alg}}(X) = \sum_{p,q} (-1)^{p - q}\, h^{p,q}(X)$.
This is the signed Hodge sum appearing in the HKR--Caldararu formula;
on compact K\"ahler $X$ one has $(-1)^{p-q} = (-1)^{p+q}$, so
$\mathrm{HH}_{\mathrm{alg}}(X) = \sum_p (-1)^p \chi_p(X) = \chi_{\mathrm{top}}(X)$.
\end{itemize}

The Theorem~\ref{thm:kappa-hodge-supertrace-identification} below shows
that $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ is the \emph{supertrace} $\Xi(X)$: the
numerical value $\chi(\cO_X)$, but computed from the
chiral/categorical side through the $(0,q)$ anti-holomorphic column
without any appeal to Kodaira vanishing or Serre cancellation. Only
after that identification is established does the Serre
anti-symmetry $h^{0,q} = h^{0,d-q}$ intervene to forbid the
identification $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = \chi(\cO_X)$ at odd $d$.

\subsection{Running examples and their Hodge columns}
\label{subsec:cy-strat-examples}

For every CY manifold below, we record $d$, the column
$(h^{0,0}, h^{0,1}, \ldots, h^{0,d})$, and the topological Euler
characteristic. The engine \texttt{cy\_d\_kappa\_d3.py} supplies these
numerically.

\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{lccccc}
\toprule

K3 surface & $2$ & $(1, 0, 1)$ & $24$ & $2$ & $\PhiFA_d$ \\
Abelian surface $E \times E$ & $2$ & $(1, 2, 1)$ & $0$ & $0$ & $\PhiFA_d$ \\
Bielliptic surface & $2$ & $(1, 1, 0)$ & $0$ & $0$ & $\PhiFA_d$ \\
Quintic threefold & $3$ & $(1, 0, 0, 1)$ & $-200$ & $0$ & $\PhiFA_d$ \\
$\mathrm{K3} \times E$ & $3$ & $(1, 1, 1, 1)$ & $0$ & $0$ & $\PhiFA_d$ \\
$E^{3}$ & $3$ & $(1, 3, 3, 1)$ & $0$ & $0$ & $\PhiFA_d$ \\
Local $\P^2$ = $\mathrm{Tot}(\omega_{\P^2})$ & $3$ & open toric column &
$\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(\P^2) = 3$ & $3/2$ & $\PhiFA_d$ \\

-- & $1$ & $\PhiFA_d$ \\
CY$_4$ sextic $\subset \P^5$ & $4$ & $(1, 0, 0, 0, 1)$ & $2610$ & $2$ & $\PhiFA_d$ \\
Septic CY$_5$ $X_7 \subset \P^6$ & $5$ & $(1, 0, 0, 0, 0, 1)$ &; & $0$ & $\PhiFA_d$ \\
$\mathrm{K3} \times X_5$ (quintic) & $5$ & $(1, 0, 1, 1, 0, 1)$ & $0$ & $0$ & $\PhiFA_d$ \\
Generic strict CY$_5$ & $5$ & $(1, 0, 0, 0, 0, 1)$ &; & $0$ & $\PhiFA_d$ \\
\midrule
\multicolumn{6}{l}{\small\itshape Specialisation-side invariants (not $\PhiFA_d$ outputs):} \\
$\kappa_{\mathrm{cat}}(X) = \chi(\cO_X)$ & all $d$ & manifold & -- & (topological) & variety \\
$\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\Phi_N) = c_N(0)/2$ & $3$ & Borcherds & -- & (automorphic) & $\SpCh$ \\
$\kappa_{\mathrm{fiber}}$ & $3$ & fibre rank & -- & (lattice) & $\SpCh$ cycle \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
\noindent
\textit{Column key}: ``Side'' records whether the invariant is native to
the $\PhiFA_d$ factorisation-algebra stage (all rows above the rule) or
to the $\SpCh_{\Sigma_{d-1},C}$ specialisation stage (rows below).
$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ values above are computed via the Hodge supertrace
$\Xi(X) = \sum_q(-1)^q h^{0,q}(X)$
(Theorem~\ref{thm:kappa-hodge-supertrace-identification}); they do not
depend on which $\SpCh$ specialisation is chosen. The three
specialisation-side invariants are construction-dependent: they change
when the Borcherds lift or fibre changes, while $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$
does not.

These values are the skeleton of the stratification theorem. The
$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ column is populated by K\"unneth multiplicativity of
the Hodge supertrace,
$\Xi(X \times Y) = \Xi(X) \cdot \Xi(Y)$ (the value at $t = -1$ of the
Poincar\'e polynomial of the $h^{0,\bullet}$ column is a homomorphism from
products of compact K\"ahler manifolds to $\Z$), and by the independent
supertrace evaluation for non-product families (quintic, local $\P^2$,
sextic). On the product entries of the table above at least one factor
carries $\Xi = 0$, so K\"unneth multiplicativity and the naive additive
rule $\Xi(X \times Y) = \Xi(X) + \Xi(Y)$ happen to give the same value; the
two diverge on products with both factors even-dimensional and
supertrace-nonzero (e.g., $\mathrm{K3} \times \mathrm{K3}$: multiplicative
$2 \cdot 2 = 4$, additive $2 + 2 = 4$ coincides; $\mathrm{K3} \times
\mathrm{K3}^{[2]}$: multiplicative $2 \cdot 3 = 6$, additive $2 + 3 = 5$
disagrees). The coincidences $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = \chi(\cO_X)$ hold only
for $(d, h^{1,0}) = (2, 0)$: K3 sits alone as the honest match.

\section{The Hodge supertrace identification}
\label{sec:cy-strat-hodge-supertrace}

\begin{theorem}[\texorpdfstring{$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$}{kappa-ch} is the Hodge supertrace of the
\texorpdfstring{$h^{0,\bullet}$}{h-0,.} column]
\label{thm:kappa-hodge-supertrace-identification}
The Hodge supertrace is an unconditional Hodge-theoretic invariant. Its
interpretation as \(\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}\) is asserted only on the constructed
\(\Phi_d\)-locus, in particular on the CY-A$_d$ locus for \(d\geq4\).
Let $X$ be a compact Calabi--Yau manifold of complex dimension $d$ and
let $\cA_X = \Phi_d(D^b(\Coh(X)))$ be the chiral algebra of
Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3} (for $d = 3$) or the analogous CY-A$_d$
construction (for other $d$, conditional for $d\geq4$). Then, on that
constructed locus,
\[
 \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\cA_X) \;=\; \Xi(X) \;=\;
 \sum_{q = 0}^{d} (-1)^q\, h^{0,q}(X),
\]
while \(\Xi(X)\) itself is an unconditional Hodge-theoretic supertrace.
At $d = 2$
with $h^{1,0}(X) = 0$ the right-hand side collapses to $\chi(\cO_X)$,
recovering Proposition~\ref{prop:cy-kappa-d2}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The argument proceeds in three steps.

\emph{Step 1. HKR and the CY trace.} By
Theorem~\ref{thm:hkr-cy}, the Hochschild homology of $D^b(\Coh(X))$
decomposes as
\[
 \mathrm{HH}_\bullet(D^b(\Coh(X))) \;\simeq\;
 \bigoplus_{p + q = \bullet}\, H^q(X, \Omega^p_X),
\]
intertwining the Connes $B$-operator on the left with the de Rham
differential on the right up to the Caldararu twist. The CY structure
determines the negative cyclic lift $\HC^-_d(D^b(\Coh(X)))$ of the
trace class, which is the $S^d$-framing datum required by
$\Phi_d$.

\emph{Step 2. The shadow scalar from $\HC^-_d$.} The Vol~I shadow
construction evaluates the degree-two shadow $S_2$ of $\cA_X$ on the
composition $\HC^-_d \to \mathrm{HH}_\bullet \to
\bigoplus_{p+q} H^q(\Omega^p)$. The $r$-matrix on $\cA_X$ is the Mukai
pairing (Proposition~\ref{prop:serre-duality-cy}), which is graded by
the Hodge filtration $F^\bullet$ on $\mathrm{HH}_\bullet$. The
$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ scalar is by construction the value of $S_2$ on
the first graded piece
$F^0/F^{-1} \cong \bigoplus_q H^q(X, \cO_X)$,
the structure-sheaf column $(0, \bullet)$. Higher Hodge columns
$H^q(X, \Omega^p_X)$ with $p \geq 1$ contribute to the full categorical
trace on $\HH_\bullet$ but enter $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ only through the
graded projection to $F^0/F^{-1}$; in CY dimension $d$ the Hodge symmetry
$h^{p, q} = h^{q, p}$ makes the $(0, q)$ supertrace equal to the full
$\chi(\cO_X)$ when the middle $h^{1, 0}, h^{2, 0}, \ldots$ vanish, but
it is the $(0, \bullet)$ filtered piece that appears in the chiral shadow,
not the full Hochschild class.

\emph{Step 3. Supertrace through the $(0,q)$ column.} The surviving
$p = 0$ column contributes $H^q(X, \cO_X)$ with sign $(-1)^q$, the
cohomological sign from the chiral bar complex. Summing over $q$,
$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\cA_X) = \sum_q (-1)^q h^{0,q}(X) = \Xi(X)$.

The three steps together establish the supertrace identity
unconditionally at $\Phi$-generic parameters. At $d = 2$ with
$h^{1,0} = 0$ the column $(1, 0, 1)$ gives $\Xi(\mathrm{K3}) = 2 =
\chi(\cO_{\mathrm{K3}})$ and we recover Proposition
\ref{prop:cy-kappa-d2}. Outside that scope $\chi(\cO_X) = 0$ is forced
by Serre (Lemma~\ref{lem:chi-O-vanishes-odd-d}), and the same
supertrace identity gives
$\Xi(X)=\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\cA_X)=0$ on compact odd-dimensional CY
inputs.  Non-zero odd-dimensional scalars belong to distinct shadow,
BCOV, Heisenberg, or BKM lanes, not to the compact Hodge supertrace.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Stage-$1$ supertrace scope: \texorpdfstring{$\kch$}{kappa-ch}
vs.\ \texorpdfstring{$\kchBV$}{kappa-ch,BV}]
\label{rem:kappa-ch-vs-kappa-chBV-scope}
Theorem~\ref{thm:kappa-hodge-supertrace-identification} identifies
$\kch(\cA_X)$ with the Hodge supertrace $\Xi(X)$ of the
$h^{0, \bullet}$ column; this is the Stage-$1$ shadow on
$\PhiFA_d(\cC)$ against ordinary $H^q(X, \cO_X)$. It does \emph{not}
subsume the one-loop BCOV curving coefficient $\kchBV$, which lives
on a distinct cohomology theory; $H^1(X, \cO_X)$ as the dilaton
cocycle $\mathrm{tr}\,\mathrm{At}(T_X)$ on compact $X$, or on
non-compact $X$ as the $\zeta$-regularised $\beta\gamma$ Casimir
energy on the boundary link $\partial X$ at $c = -2$; and
sources from the Costello--Li holomorphic BV action
(\texttt{arXiv:1606.00365}) rather than from $\Xi$. On compact
CY$_d$ where the actual dilaton class vanishes (the quintic, the four
$d = 4$ families of Section~\ref{sec:cy-strat-d4-explicit}, and the
$d = 5$ families of Section~\ref{sec:cy-strat-d5-explicit}), the
dilaton cocycle
$\mathrm{tr}\, \mathrm{At}(T_X)$ vanishes in $H^1(X, \cO_X)$ and
$\kchBV = 0$, so the two invariants coincide numerically by zero.
For $K3\times E$ and $E^3$, $h^{0,1}$ is respectively $1$ and $3$;
the compact Hodge supertrace still vanishes by Serre/Künneth, but the
argument is not an $h^{0,1}=0$ argument.
The scope separation is substantive on the non-compact CY$_3$
frontier where the boundary link $\partial X$ supports a nontrivial
$\beta\gamma$-ghost zero mode; e.g.\ the conifold with link
$T^{1,1} \simeq S^2 \times S^3$, and local $\P^2$ with link
$S^5/\mathbb Z_3$; so $\kchBV \neq 0$ through the boundary-link
$\zeta$-regularised Casimir pairing. The canonical conifold row
$(+1, 0, +1, -1)$ of
Theorem~\ref{thm:conifold-polyakov-ghost-balance} exhibits the
non-trivial balance $\kch = -\kchBV$ \emph{per the Polyakov
$c = -2$ ghost-cancellation identity} on $\mathsf G$-class
non-compact CY$_3$ with single compact curve class; the two
invariants remain two separate Stage-$1$ data evaluated against two
distinct cohomology theories.
\end{remark}

\begin{lemma}[Serre-enforced vanishing of \texorpdfstring{$\chi(\cO_X)$}{chi(O-X)} at odd \texorpdfstring{$d$}{d}]
\label{lem:chi-O-vanishes-odd-d}
For any compact Calabi--Yau $X$ of odd complex dimension $d$,
$\chi(\cO_X) = 0$. The proof is Serre pairwise cancellation:
$h^{0,q}(X) = h^{0, d-q}(X)$ and, for $d$ odd, $(-1)^q + (-1)^{d-q} = 0$
for every pair $(q, d-q)$ with $q \neq d - q$; there is no middle term.
See Griffiths--Harris, \emph{Principles of Algebraic Geometry}, Ch.~0
and the engine routine \texttt{cy\_d\_kappa\_d3.chi\_O\_vanishes\_odd\_d}.
\end{lemma}

\begin{remark}[Why the identification \texorpdfstring{$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} =
\chi(\cO_X)$}{kappa-ch = chi(O-X)} survives only at \texorpdfstring{$(d, h^{1,0}) = (2, 0)$}{(d, h-1,0) = (2, 0)}]
\label{rem:why-only-k3}
The supertrace $\Xi(X)$ and the number $\chi(\cO_X)$ are
computationally identical (both are $\sum_q (-1)^q h^{0,q}$). The
distinction is that the supertrace is a well-defined invariant of the
CY category (it is the degree-two shadow $S_2(\cA_X)$ passing through
$\HC^-_d$), whereas the phrase ``$\chi(\cO_X)$'' invokes Serre cancellation
whenever $d$ is odd. At $E$ with $(d, h^{0,\bullet}) = (1, (1, 1))$ the
Serre pairing $h^{0,0} = h^{0,1} = 1$ cancels the two contributions in
the written-out sum, so $\Xi(E) = 1 - 1 = 0 = \chi(\cO_E)$; the
independent chiral computation $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\cA_E) = 0$ agrees,
coming from the abelian $\pi_1$-cohomology of $E$, not from a pairing
with the vacuum. Supertrace and chiral calculation agree ($0 = 0$);
the $E$ case exhibits the supertrace as a \emph{cancellation}, not an
\emph{equality} between nonzero pieces. The only case where no cancellation occurs --
every nonzero entry of the $h^{0,\bullet}$ column lies at even $q$ --
is K3. This is the structural reason $(d, h^{1,0}) = (2, 0)$ is
singled out.
\end{remark}

\section{Dimension-by-dimension stratification}
\label{sec:cy-strat-stratification}

The Hodge-filtered supertrace formula
\textup{(}Theorem~\ref{thm:kappa-hodge-supertrace-identification}\textup{)}
collapses the entire CY-D landscape into three structural strata,
discriminated by the parity of $d$ and by the presence of a
holomorphic-symplectic form. This unifies Theorem~B at $d = 2$
\textup{(}K$3$\textup{),} the $d = 3$ case study
$K3 \times E$, the $d = 4$ sextic and hyperk\"ahler examples, the
$d = 5$ Universal Serre Cancellation, and every higher-dimensional
example below.

\begin{theorem}[CY-D tri-stratum theorem]
\label{thm:cy-d-tri-stratum}
Let $X$ be a compact CY$_d$ manifold with Hodge column
$h^{0, \bullet}(X) = (h^{0, 0}, h^{0, 1}, \ldots, h^{0, d})$. The
Hodge-filtered supertrace
$\Xi(X) := \sum_{q = 0}^d (-1)^q h^{0, q}(X)$
takes one of three forms, determined by the parity of $d$ and the
holomorphic-symplectic structure of $X$:
\begin{enumerate}
 \item[\textup{(I)}] \emph{Odd $d$ stratum.} If $d$ is odd, then
 $\Xi(X) = 0$ for every compact CY$_d$. Mechanism: Serre duality
 $h^{0, q} = h^{0, d - q}$ pairs every term with its sign-opposite,
 and $d$ odd means there is no middle term ($q = d/2$ is
 non-integral); pairs cancel uniformly.
 \item[\textup{(II)}] \emph{Even $d$ strict-CY stratum.} If $d$ is even
 and $X$ has holonomy exactly $\mathrm{SU}(d)$ \textup{(}so
 $h^{0, k} = 0$ for $0 < k < d$; the unique global holomorphic
 form is the Calabi--Yau volume\textup{),} then
 $\Xi(X) = 2$. Examples: sextic fourfold in $\mathbb{P}^5$,
 decic $5$-fold in $\mathbb{P}(1^5, 5)$, and every strict CY of
 even dimension.
 \item[\textup{(III)}] \emph{Even $d$ holomorphic-symplectic stratum.}
 If $d$ is even and $X$ is irreducible holomorphic-symplectic
 \textup{(}holonomy $\mathrm{Sp}(d/2)$, with
 $h^{0, k} = 1$ for every even $0 \leq k \leq d$ and
 $h^{0, k} = 0$ for $k$ odd\textup{),} then
 $\Xi(X) = (d/2) + 1$. In particular: K$3^{[n]}$ at
 $d = 2n$ has $\Xi = n + 1$, recovering $\Xi(K3) = 2$ at $n = 1$
 and $\Xi(K3^{[2]}) = 3$ at $n = 2$.
\end{enumerate}
Strata \textup{(II)} and \textup{(III)} overlap precisely when
$\mathrm{SU}(d) = \mathrm{Sp}(d/2)$, which occurs only at $d = 2$
\textup{(}where $\mathrm{SU}(2) = \mathrm{Sp}(1)$; both strata assign
$\Xi(K3) = 2$\textup{).} For $d \geq 4$ the strata are disjoint, and
together with \textup{(I)} they exhaust the Beauville--Bogomolov
classification of compact CY holonomies on connected simply-connected
$X$ with $h^{1, 0} = 0$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Stratum (I) is direct Serre cancellation: for $d$ odd,
$h^{0, q} = h^{0, d - q}$ pairs each $q < d/2$ with $d - q > d/2$,
contributing $(-1)^q + (-1)^{d - q} = (-1)^q + (-1)^{d - q}$;
since $(-1)^{d - q} = (-1)^d (-1)^{-q} = -(-1)^q$ for $d$ odd, each
pair contributes $0$. No middle term exists since $d/2$ is
non-integral.

Stratum (II) uses the strict-CY definition: for $X$ with holonomy exactly
$\mathrm{SU}(d)$, the holonomy representation on $\Omega^q$ has no trivial
summand for $0 < q < d$, so $h^{0, q} = 0$ in this range. The only
nonzero $h^{0, q}$ are $q = 0$ (constants) and $q = d$ (the Calabi--Yau
volume form). A second holomorphic form at $q = d/2$ would reduce the
holonomy to $\mathrm{Sp}(d/2)$, placing $X$ in stratum \textup{(III)}
instead. Hence $\Xi(X) = h^{0, 0} + (-1)^d h^{0, d} = 1 + 1 = 2$, since
$d$ is even.

Stratum (III) uses the holomorphic-symplectic Hodge structure of
Beauville--Bogomolov: for $X$ irreducible holomorphic-symplectic of
complex dimension $d = 2n$, the holomorphic-symplectic form $\sigma$ and
its powers $\sigma^k$ for $k = 0, 1, \ldots, n$ generate the only nonzero
$h^{0, q}$ with $q$ even. Specifically $h^{0, 2k} = 1$ for $0 \leq k \leq n$
and $h^{0, q} = 0$ for $q$ odd. The supertrace is
$\Xi = \sum_{k = 0}^n h^{0, 2k} = n + 1 = (d/2) + 1$.

Mutual exclusivity follows from Beauville--Bogomolov: every compact
K\"ahler CY manifold with $c_1 = 0$ admits a finite \'etale cover that
decomposes as $T^d \times \prod_i \mathrm{HK}_i \times \prod_j Y_j$
where $T^d$ is a torus, $\mathrm{HK}_i$ are irreducible holomorphic-symplectic,
and $Y_j$ are strict CYs. Restricting to $h^{1, 0} = 0$ \textup{(}the
universal hypothesis on the present chapter\textup{)} eliminates the
torus factor; restricting to one Bogomolov factor gives one of the three
strata exclusively.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[The \texorpdfstring{$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$}{kappa-ch} landscape has structure only at
 even \texorpdfstring{$d$}{d}]
\label{rem:kappa-ch-landscape-even-d}
Stratum (I) collapses the entire odd-$d$ landscape to the single value
$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\cA_X) = 0$. At odd $d$, every algebraic richness
of the chiral output $\cA_X$ \textup{(}detected through OTHER invariants
such as shadow-tower depth, BCOV partition function, MO-stable-envelope
R-matrix, etc.\textup{)} is invisible to $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ alone.
The rich $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ landscape lives entirely in even $d$,
distributed across strata (II) and (III) by the holomorphic-symplectic
form.

This is the structural reason that the K$3$ chiral algebra at $d = 2$ is
the canonical exemplar: the two stratifying formulas \emph{agree} on K$3$.
Stratum (III) gives $\Xi = (d/2) + 1 = 2$ at $d = 2$, $n = 1$. Stratum (II)
with the K$3$ column $h^{0, \bullet}(\mathrm{K}3) = (1, 0, 1)$ and the
$h^{0, d/2} = h^{0, 1} = 0$ middle term gives
$\Xi = 1 + (-1)^{1} \cdot 0 + 1 = 2$. Both routes land on the same value
because K$3$ is simultaneously strict CY (holonomy $\mathrm{SU}(2)$) and
irreducible holomorphic-symplectic (holonomy $\mathrm{Sp}(1) = \mathrm{SU}(2)$),
an accident of low dimension: $\mathrm{SU}(2) = \mathrm{Sp}(1)$. In higher
even dimension the two holonomy classes are distinct and the two
stratum formulas diverge, with stratum (II) giving $2$ (at vanishing
middle holomorphic form) and stratum (III) giving $n + 1$ for K$3^{[n]}$.
\end{remark}

\begin{corollary}[BCOV \texorpdfstring{$F_g$}{F-g} vanishing across all even \texorpdfstring{$d$}{d}]
\label{cor:bcov-fg-vanishing-all-even-d}
Theorems~\ref{thm:bcov-f2-zero-correction-d4} \textup{(}$d = 4$\textup{)}
and~\ref{thm:bcov-fg-zero-correction-d5} \textup{(}$d = 5$\textup{)} are
the chain-level expressions of Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-d-tri-stratum}: the
BCOV $F_g$ holomorphic anomaly equation respects the underlying Serre
symmetry of stratum (I) and the holomorphic-symplectic Hodge structure of
stratum (III), so the supertrace is the COMPLETE answer in both strata.
At stratum (II), the BCOV $F_g$ contribution is similarly forced to zero
by the constancy $F_1 = \chi/24$ on the BTT moduli of strict CYs.
\end{corollary}

\section{Shadow-class stratification by CY holonomy}
\label{sec:cy-d-shadow-class-stratification}

Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-d-tri-stratum} controls the scalar $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\cA_X)$
by the Hodge column $h^{0, \bullet}$. A complementary, logically
independent question is: what is the \emph{shadow class}
$\mathrm{Cl}(\cA_X) \in \{G, L, C, M\}$ of the chiral algebra
$\cA_X = \Phi_d(D^b(\Coh(X)))$ per stratum? The shadow class is
determined by the higher shadow coefficients $(S_3, S_4, \ldots)$
(the OPE-pole structure of the chiral $r$-matrix), not by $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = S_2$
alone. The classification below uses the Vol~I shadow-depth
hierarchy $G < L < C < M$ indexed by $r_{\max} \in \{2, 3, 4, \infty\}$
together with the Vol~III closed $\kappa_\bullet$ menu.

\begin{theorem}[CY-D shadow-class stratification by holonomy]
\label{thm:cy-d-shadow-class-stratification}
The statement is read at the level of the tree-level Hodge-supertrace
input to the shadow tower. It is supplied unconditionally for
\(d\in\{1,2,3\}\) by CY-A$_2$ and by the infinity-categorical CY-A$_3$
resolution of Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3}; for \(d\geq4\) it
assumes the CY-A$_d$ construction. The BCOV \(F_g\)-instanton promotion
in stratum (I$_{\mathrm{BCOV}}$) is unconditional in the cited cases but
is not a universal statement over all compact CY$_3$ manifolds.
Let $X$ be a compact CY$_d$ manifold with $h^{1, 0}(X) = 0$ and
$\cA_X = \Phi_d(D^b(\Coh(X)))$, all statements at generic parameters of
$\Phi$ and ambient-qualified per stratum below:
\begin{enumerate}
 \item[\textup{(I$_{\mathrm{tree}}$)}] \emph{Odd $d$ stratum, tree level.}
 If $d$ is odd, the Hodge-supertrace input to the shadow tower is
 $S_2=\Xi(X)=0$ by Serre pairwise cancellation.  This statement does
 not compute the higher shadow coefficients $S_3,S_4,\ldots$; those
 require OPE/BRST/BCOV input beyond the linear Hodge supertrace.  The
 tree-level shadow class is therefore $G$ only on the additional
 vanishing locus for the higher tree coefficients.
 \item[\textup{(I$_{\mathrm{BCOV}}$)}] \emph{Odd $d$ stratum, BCOV-promoted.}
 At compact odd-$d$ CY with nontrivial Gopakumar--Vafa spectrum, the
 BCOV instanton corrections promote the shadow class to $M$
 \textup{(}depth $r_{\max} = \infty$\textup{)}. The quintic
 $X_5 \subset \mathbb{P}^4$ realizes this promotion: classical
 $S_4 = 0$ gives tree-level class $L$ once the $\sigma_3 = 5$
 triple-intersection Yukawa is retained, but the Gopakumar--Vafa
 genus-zero invariants $n^0_d$ are nonzero for every $d \geq 1$,
 driving $S_4^{\mathrm{instanton}} \neq 0$ and hence class $M$
 \textup{(}Proposition~\ref{prop:quintic-shadow-coefficients}, with
 the quintic $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{BCOV}} = \chi/24 = -25/3$
 separately; see Remark~\ref{rem:kappa-ch-quintic-two-views}
 below for the supertrace-vs-BCOV disambiguation\textup{)}.
 \item[\textup{(II)}] \emph{Even $d$, strict CY stratum.}
 If $X$ has holonomy exactly $\mathrm{SU}(d)$ \textup{(}strict CY,
 $h^{0, k} = 0$ for $0 < k < d$\textup{),} then the tree-level
 algebra $\cA_X$ is the rank-$2$ lattice VOA on the holomorphic
 $(0, 0)$ and $(0, d)$ sector \textup{(}the vacuum and the
 Calabi--Yau volume form\textup{),} which is a free-field algebra.
 Free-field algebras have $S_3 = S_4 = 0$ and shadow class $G$ with
 $r_{\max} = 2$. Sextic $X_6 \subset \mathbb{P}^5$ and decic
 $\subset \mathbb{P}(1^5, 5)$ \textup{(}with $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = 2$\textup{)}
 are canonical class-$G$ representatives.
 \item[\textup{(III)}] \emph{Even $d = 2n$, holomorphic-symplectic stratum.}
 If $X$ is irreducible holomorphic-symplectic \textup{(}holonomy
 $\mathrm{Sp}(n)$\textup{),} then $h^{0, 2k} = 1$ for
 $0 \leq k \leq n$ and the tree-level algebra $\cA_X$ is the
 rank-$(n + 1)$ lattice VOA generated by the $\sigma^k$ tower
 \textup{(}the $n + 1$ powers of the holomorphic-symplectic form\textup{).}
 This is a free-field algebra: $S_3 = S_4 = 0$, shadow class $G$,
 $r_{\max} = 2$. K3 \textup{(}$n = 1$\textup{),} $\mathrm{K3}^{[n]}$,
 and the Beauville--Donagi Fano variety of lines $F(Y)$ on a cubic
 fourfold are canonical class-$G$ representatives, with
 $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = n + 1$. At $n = 1$ \textup{(}K3\textup{),}
 strata \textup{(II)} and \textup{(III)} coincide and both yield
 class $G$ on the $\Phi_2$-output vertex algebra, consistent with
 the Mukai lattice VOA computation
 \textup{(}Chapter~\ref{ch:k3-chiral-algebra},
 $S_3 = S_4 = 0 \Rightarrow \Delta = 0 \Rightarrow$ class $G$\textup{).}
\end{enumerate}
The tree-level shadow class is thus uniformly $G$ across every compact
CY$_d$ stratum \textup{(}I$_{\mathrm{tree}}$\textup{), (II), (III).}
Promotion to higher shadow classes requires either
\textup{(a)} compact odd-$d$ BCOV $F_g$ instanton corrections
\textup{(I$_{\mathrm{BCOV}}$)},
\textup{(b)} non-compact toric promotion via McKay quiver
$\mathbb{Z}/N$-symmetry \textup{(}see
Theorem~\ref{thm:local-p2-shadow}: local $\mathbb{P}^2$ is class $M$
with $r_{\max} = \infty$ by $\mathbb{Z}/3$-McKay\textup{),} or
\textup{(c)} K3 \emph{sigma-model} enhancement of the $\Phi_2$-output
lattice VOA to an $\widehat{\fsu}_2^{\oplus n}$ subalgebra at level $1$
\textup{(}Kummer point, Chapter~\ref{ch:k3-chiral-algebra}: enhanced
subalgebra is class $L$ with $r_{\max} = 3$\textup{).}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
We prove each stratum separately.

\emph{Stratum (I$_{\mathrm{tree}}$).} At odd $d$ with $h^{1, 0} = 0$,
the Hodge column $(1, h^{0, 1}, \ldots, h^{0, 1}, 1)$ paired by
Serre produces, at tree level (before BCOV $F_g$ corrections), a
free-field lattice VOA structure on $\cA_X$: the paired generators
$\{1, \omega_{d}\}$ at degrees $0$ and $d$, together with the Serre-dual
pairs $\{\eta_q, \eta_{d - q}\}$ at intermediate degrees
$q \in \{1, \ldots, d - 1\}$, mutually commute at tree level because
\textup{(a)} Serre duality pairs $\eta_q$ with $\eta_{d - q}$ in a
degree-zero pairing $\langle \eta_q, \eta_{d - q}\rangle = \int_X
\eta_q \wedge \eta_{d - q} = \mathrm{vol}_X$, giving a symplectic
($d$ odd, so $\eta$-pair is in antisymmetric position) rank-$2$
block per pair, and \textup{(b)} cross-pairs between distinct
$q, q'$ vanish by bigrading conservation. The total structure is a
rank-$(d + 1)$ symplectic lattice VOA, which is free-field
(Frenkel--Ben-Zvi \emph{Vertex algebras and algebraic curves} 2001
Chapter 3, free-field lattice VOA construction). Free-field
lattice VOAs have $S_3 = S_4 = 0$ (no cubic or quartic OPE pole;
every OPE has at most a double pole by free-field Wick contraction),
so $\Delta = 8 \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} S_4 = 0$ and the shadow class is
$G$ with $r_{\max} = 2$. Equivalently: $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = 0$
from the Serre-cancellation of $\Xi(X)$ (stratum (I) of
Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-d-tri-stratum}), and the higher shadow
coefficients are zero from the free-field structure. The two inputs
(vanishing scalar; free-field higher) converge on class $G$.

\emph{Stratum (I$_{\mathrm{BCOV}}$).} At compact odd-$d$ CY with
nontrivial Gopakumar--Vafa spectrum, the BCOV holomorphic-anomaly
equation (Bershadsky--Cecotti--Ooguri--Vafa 1994, Theorem~11.1)
propagates to the shadow tower via
$S_4^{\mathrm{instanton}} = \sum_{d \geq 1} n^0_d / \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{BCOV}}$
(Proposition~\ref{prop:quintic-shadow-coefficients}, clause (iii)).
For the quintic: $n^0_1 = 2875 \neq 0$ (Candelas--de la Ossa--Green--Parkes 1991),
forcing $S_4^{\mathrm{instanton}} \neq 0$ and hence class $M$ with
$r_{\max} = \infty$. The class-$M$ conclusion applies conditionally:
\textup{(i)} to the quintic unconditionally, via the explicit nonzero
$n^0_1 = 2875$ computation; \textup{(ii)} to compact non-product CY$_3$
families with a nonvanishing leading Gopakumar--Vafa invariant at
degree~$1$ or at some higher degree for which the BCOV mirror-symmetry
series is known to be nontrivial (this is proved case by case; universal
nonvanishing of the GV spectrum for every compact non-product CY$_3$ is
an open conjecture and is not claimed here). The open frontier of
Remark~\ref{rem:cy-strat-open-frontier} is precisely the set of compact
non-product non-toric CY$_3$ for which the GV-nonvanishing input is not
independently established.

This BCOV promotion is not a chiral Booth--Lazarev vanishing theorem.
For the quintic it proves the first LCS obstruction coefficient
$S_{4,1}^{\mathrm{inst}}=2875/(-25/3)=-345$ and hence the leading sign
$(\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{BCOV}})^3S_{4,1}^{\mathrm{inst}}>0$.
The residual sewing obstruction
$O_3^{\mathrm{BL}}\in\varprojlim^1_g A^{\mathrm{sew},g}$ is killed only
by a full BCOV Borel sign lemma for the entire genus tower. The
Ran-space obstruction
$O_1^{\mathrm{BL}}\in H^1(\Ran(C),\mathrm{Aut}_\otimes(\mathrm{Gen}_{\mathrm{BL}}))$
is a separate Smith-recognition complex. Neither complex is nullhomotopic
from the scalar shadow calculation alone.

\emph{Stratum (II).} At strict CY$_d$ with holonomy exactly
$\mathrm{SU}(d)$ (even $d$), the nontrivial Hodge column is
$\{h^{0, 0}, h^{0, d}\} = \{1, 1\}$ with $h^{0, k} = 0$ for
$0 < k < d$. The chiral algebra $\cA_X$ at tree level is generated by
the $H^0(X, \cO_X) = \mathbb{C}$ vacuum $|0\rangle$ and the
$H^d(X, \cO_X) = \mathbb{C}\omega$ volume form, giving a Fock-module
structure of Heisenberg type: the pair $(|0\rangle, \omega)$ generates
a rank-$1$ Heisenberg VOA $\mathcal{H}_1$ via the mode expansion of
$\omega(z) = \sum_n \omega_n z^{-n - 1}$ with canonical commutation
$[\omega_m, \omega_n] = m \delta_{m + n, 0}$ (the standard Heisenberg
relation, Frenkel--Ben-Zvi 2001 Proposition~2.2.1). The shadow tower
of a Heisenberg VOA $\mathcal{H}_k$ has $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = k$,
$S_3 = 0$ (no cubic pole: Heisenberg OPE is
$\omega(z) \omega(w) \sim k / (z - w)^2$, a pure double pole with no
cubic term), and $S_4 = 0$ (no quartic pole either). The critical
discriminant $\Delta = 8 \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} S_4 = 0$ gives class $G$
with $r_{\max} = 2$ (Vol~I shadow-class-$G$ criterion: $S_3 = S_4 = 0
\Leftrightarrow \Delta = 0$ and shadow tower terminates at degree $2$).
The Hodge supertrace $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = \Xi(X) = 2$ from Stratum (II)
of Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-d-tri-stratum} identifies the Heisenberg level
$k = 2$. The sextic $X_6 \subset \mathbb{P}^5$, decic $\subset
\mathbb{P}(1^5, 5)$, and every higher even-$d$ strict CY realize this
Heisenberg pattern at tree level.

\emph{Stratum (III).} At irreducible holomorphic-symplectic $X$ of
complex dimension $d = 2n$, the Hodge column has $h^{0, 2k} = 1$ for
$0 \leq k \leq n$ and $h^{0, q} = 0$ otherwise (Beauville--Bogomolov
decomposition). The chiral algebra $\cA_X$ at tree level is generated
by the $n + 1$ classes $\sigma^k \in H^{0, 2k}(X, \mathbb{C})$
($k = 0, \ldots, n$) with corresponding bosonic modes
$\sigma^k(z) = \sum_m \sigma^k_m z^{-m - 1}$. The Fujiki relations
(Fujiki \emph{Hodge structures on hyperk\"ahler manifolds}, Publ.~Inst.
Math.\ IHES 1987, cubic Fujiki constant $c_X$) give the pairing
$\int_X \sigma^k \wedge \bar\sigma^{n - k} = c_X \cdot \mathrm{vol}(X)$
pairwise, and orthogonality for distinct Hodge weights $(k, k')$ with
$k + k' \neq n$. The induced mode commutator is
$[\sigma^k_m, \sigma^{n - k}_p] = m \cdot c_X \cdot \delta_{m + p, 0}$
(pure Heisenberg central-charge relation with $\sigma^k$ and
$\sigma^{n - k}$ forming a conjugate pair), and
$[\sigma^k_m, \sigma^{k'}_p] = 0$ for $k + k' \neq n$.
This is exactly the defining commutator of the rank-$(n + 1)$
Heisenberg VOA $\mathcal{H}^{\otimes (n + 1)}$ at level $c_X$
(equivalently: $n + 1$ mutually-commuting free-boson generators, each
a copy of $\mathcal{H}_{c_X}$). The shadow coefficients of a tensor
product of free-boson VOAs are $S_3 = S_4 = 0$ and
$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = \sum_{k = 0}^{n} c_X = (n + 1) c_X$ at the
standard normalization $c_X = 1$, recovering $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = n + 1$
from Stratum (III) of Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-d-tri-stratum}.
The critical discriminant $\Delta = 0$ gives class $G$ with
$r_{\max} = 2$. K3 ($n = 1$, rank-$2$ Heisenberg tensor),
$\mathrm{K3}^{[2]}$ ($n = 2$, rank-$3$ Heisenberg tensor), and the
Beauville--Donagi cubic-4-fold lines $F(Y) \sim_{\mathrm{deform}}
\mathrm{K3}^{[2]}$ (Beauville--Donagi 1985) all realize this pattern;
the full rank-$24$ Mukai lattice VOA on K3 of
Chapter~\ref{ch:k3-chiral-algebra} is an enlargement to the full Mukai
lattice but the class-$G$ conclusion is preserved
($S_3 = S_4 = 0 \Rightarrow \Delta = 0 \Rightarrow$ class $G$).

\emph{Overall conclusion.} The tree-level shadow class is $G$
uniformly across strata. Promotion mechanisms (a) compact odd-$d$ BCOV,
(b) non-compact toric McKay $\mathbb{Z}/N$-symmetry, (c) K3-sigma-model
enhancement are the three documented routes out of class $G$; each is
explicit in the indicated chapter or stratum.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Disambiguation: \texorpdfstring{$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Hodge}}$}{kappa-ch-Hodge} vs
\texorpdfstring{$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{BCOV}}$}{kappa-ch-BCOV} at the quintic]
\label{rem:kappa-ch-quintic-two-views}
The quintic threefold carries two distinct invariants that each go by
the name $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$; the $\Phi$-output
invariant must be distinguished from the construction-level
invariant.
\begin{enumerate}
 \item $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Hodge}}(\cA_{X_5}) = \Xi(X_5) = 0$,
 the Hodge supertrace of the $(0, \bullet)$ column
 $(1, 0, 0, 1)$: vanishes by Serre
 (Theorem~\ref{thm:kappa-hodge-supertrace-identification}).
 \item $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{BCOV}}(\cA_{X_5}) = \chi(X_5) / 24 = -25/3$,
 the BCOV $F_1$ one-loop free energy divided by the universal
 Euler coefficient: nonzero, fractional
 (Proposition~\ref{prop:quintic-shadow}).
\end{enumerate}
Both are ``$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$'' of ``the quintic chiral algebra'',
but they are invariants of \emph{different constructions}:
(1) is the supertrace trace on $\HC^-_3$ of the shadow tower of
$\cA_{X_5} = \Phi_3(D^b(\Coh(X_5)))$ (the categorical $\Phi_3$ output),
while (2) is the BCOV-genus-$1$ coefficient of the topological-string
partition function on the quintic (a separately constructed worldsheet
invariant). These two invariants are both legitimate and both denoted
$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ in the standard literature (Vol~III
\cite{VolI} at
Proposition~\ref{prop:quintic-shadow} for the BCOV route, and this
chapter Section~\ref{sec:cy-strat-stratification} for the Hodge
supertrace route), but they are not the same number and are distinct.
Six routes to $G(K3 \times E)$ are six different
constructions, not six $\Phi$ applications; the same discipline applies here:
$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Hodge}}$ and $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{BCOV}}$
are two-route analogs at the quintic.

The shadow-class stratification of
Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-d-shadow-class-stratification} uses the Hodge
supertrace input \textup{(}tree level, stratum (I$_{\mathrm{tree}}$)\textup{),}
and promotes to class $M$ via BCOV instanton corrections to $S_4$
\textup{(}stratum (I$_{\mathrm{BCOV}}$)\textup{).} The two routes
converge on the same shadow-class outcome at the quintic
\textup{(}class $M$\textup{)} but differ on the degree-$2$ scalar:
$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Hodge}} = 0$ from stratum-(I$_{\mathrm{tree}}$)
Serre cancellation, $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{BCOV}} = -25/3$ from
$F_1$-constant-map formula.
\end{remark}

\begin{corollary}[Shadow class and CY dimension: the universal compact
class-\texorpdfstring{$G$}{G} signature at tree level]
\label{cor:universal-tree-class-G}
Assume the tree-level \(\Phi_d\)-shadow is constructed in the sense of
Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-d-shadow-class-stratification}.
For every compact CY$_d$ with $h^{1, 0} = 0$, the tree-level shadow class
$\mathrm{Cl}^{\mathrm{tree}}(\cA_X)$ is $G$ uniformly across
$d \in \{2, 3, 4, 5\}$. (At $d = 1$, the $h^{1, 0} = 0$ scope is vacuous:
every elliptic curve has $h^{1, 0} = 1$, so there is no compact $d = 1$
CY with $h^{1, 0} = 0$ to classify; the $d = 1$ entry is treated
separately at Theorem~\ref{thm:kappa-stratification-by-d} via the
$\Xi(E) = 0$ Serre cancellation.)
The tree-level shadow depth is $r_{\max}^{\mathrm{tree}} = 2$
throughout the $d \in \{2, 3, 4, 5\}$ scope.
Higher shadow classes $L, C, M$ appear only through
\textup{(a)} compact odd-$d$ BCOV $F_g$ instanton promotion,
\textup{(b)} non-compact toric McKay $\mathbb{Z}/N$-enhancement, or
\textup{(c)} K3-sigma-model $\widehat{\fsu}_2^{\oplus n}$ enhancement.
Equivalently: the CY dimension $d$ alone does not determine the shadow
class; the shadow class is determined by the compactness, the holonomy,
and the presence/absence of a promotion mechanism, not by $d$ directly.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Strata (I$_{\mathrm{tree}}$), (II), (III) of
Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-d-shadow-class-stratification} each yield class $G$
at tree level, and the three strata exhaust the compact CY$_d$
landscape with $h^{1, 0} = 0$ per Beauville--Bogomolov
(Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-d-tri-stratum}). Hence class $G$ is universal at
tree level across $d$. The three documented promotion mechanisms are
enumerated in the last paragraph of
Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-d-shadow-class-stratification}; each is
conditional on auxiliary data (BCOV instanton spectrum, McKay quiver
symmetry, K3 sigma-model enhancement) beyond the raw CY dimension.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Cross-volume implication: Vol~I four-class landscape]
\label{rem:cy-d-shadow-class-cross-vol-implication}
The Vol~I four-class landscape $\{G, L, C, M\}$ is populated by four
archetype vertex algebras: Heisenberg $\mathcal{H}_k$ (class $G$),
affine Kac--Moody $V_k(\mathfrak{g})$ (class $L$), $\beta\gamma$ (class
$C$), Virasoro $\mathrm{Vir}_c$ (class $M$). The Vol~III
$\Phi_d$-output stratification of
Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-d-shadow-class-stratification} realizes class $G$
universally at tree level; classes $L, C, M$ are accessed only through
the three promotion mechanisms. In particular:
\begin{itemize}
 \item Class $L$ (affine Kac--Moody) is accessed from $\Phi_d$ only via
 the K3 $\mathcal{N} = 4$ sigma-model enhancement to
 $\widehat{\fsu}_2^{\oplus n}$ at level $1$ on the Kummer point
 (Chapter~\ref{ch:k3-chiral-algebra}).
 \item Class $C$ ($\beta\gamma$) is accessed from $\Phi_d$ only at
 non-compact local surfaces (Theorem~\ref{thm:local-p2-shadow}:
 local $\mathbb{P}^2$ via the $\beta\gamma$ boundary algebra is
 class $C$ at $r_{\max} = 4$; see also the conifold bichamber
 computation of Chapter~\ref{ch:toric-coha}).
 \item Class $M$ (Virasoro-type) is accessed via BCOV instanton
 corrections at compact odd-$d$ CY (quintic, $K3 \times E$,
 non-product CY$_3$) or via the K3 sigma-model full
 $\mathcal{N} = 4$ superconformal enhancement (Chapter~\ref{ch:k3-chiral-algebra},
 full sigma model class $M$).
\end{itemize}
This population pattern is the Vol~III-specific analog of the Vol~I
averaging-map principle: the averaging map
$\mathrm{av}: \mathfrak{g}^{E_1} \to \mathfrak{g}^{\mathrm{mod}}$
collapses every standard chiral algebra into one of four archetypes;
the $\Phi_d$-output tree-level algebra $\cA_X$ lands on the Heisenberg
archetype (class $G$) uniformly, and higher classes require ambient-category
enhancement beyond the bare $\Phi_d$ output.
\end{remark}

\begin{theorem}[Stratification of \texorpdfstring{$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$}{kappa-ch} by
\texorpdfstring{$(d, h^{0,\bullet})$}{(d, h-0,.)}]
\label{thm:kappa-stratification-by-d}
On the constructed \(\Phi_d\)-locus, with the \(d\geq4\) entries read
under CY-A$_d$, the following table is the Hodge-supertrace
stratification.
Let $X$ be a compact CY$_d$ and $\cA_X$ the associated chiral algebra.
For the standard families of Vol~III, the invariant
$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\cA_X)$ is determined by the Hodge column
$h^{0,\bullet}(X)$ via
Theorem~\ref{thm:kappa-hodge-supertrace-identification} and takes the
following values.

\textbf{$d = 1$ (elliptic curve $E$).} Column $h^{0,\bullet} = (1, 1)$,
so $\Xi(E) = 1 - 1 = 0$ and $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\cA_E) = 0$. Two Heisenberg invariants must be
distinguished: the shadow scalar $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ of the chiral
output $\cA_E = \Phi_1(D^b(\Coh(E)))$, which vanishes by the supertrace
$1 - 1 = 0$; and the Heisenberg level $\kappa_{\mathrm{Heis}} = k$, which
parametrizes the free-boson lattice VOA $H_k$ of rank $1$ and equals $1$
at the basic representation $H_1$. The lattice VOA $H_1$ is a separate
construction from $\Phi_1(D^b(\Coh(E)))$, so $\kappa_{\mathrm{Heis}}(H_1)
= 1$ and $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\Phi_1(D^b(\Coh(E)))) = 0$ are invariants
of different algebras. In this chapter $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ always
denotes the supertrace on the $\Phi_d$ output; the $d = 1$ value is
$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\Phi_1(D^b(\Coh(E)))) = 0$.

\textbf{$d = 2$, K3 ($h^{1,0} = 0$).} Column $(1, 0, 1)$, so
$\Xi(K3) = 2$ and $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\cA_{K3}) = 2 = \chi(\cO_{K3})$.
This is the honest case where the supertrace and the naive
$\chi(\cO_X)$ agree.

\textbf{$d = 2$, abelian surface.} Column $(1, 2, 1)$, so
$\Xi(E \times E) = 1 - 2 + 1 = 0$ and
$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\cA_{E\times E}) = 0$. The cancellation is
pairwise: the two $h^{0,1}$ forms subtract exactly from
$h^{0,0} + h^{0,2} = 2$.

\textbf{$d = 2$, bielliptic surface.} Column $(1, 1, 0)$, so
$\Xi(\mathrm{biell}) = 1 - 1 + 0 = 0$ and
$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\cA_{\mathrm{biell}}) = 0$. No holomorphic
$2$-form; $h^{1,0} = 1$ forces a cancellation at $q = 1$.

\textbf{$d = 3$, generic quintic.} Column $(1, 0, 0, 1)$, so
$\Xi(X_5) = 1 - 0 + 0 - 1 = 0$ and
$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\cA_{X_5}) = 0$.

\textbf{$d = 3$, $\mathrm{K3} \times E$.} Kunneth gives the column
$(1, 1, 1, 1)$: $h^{0,0} = 1$, $h^{0,1} = h^{0,0}_{K3} \cdot h^{0,1}_E
+ h^{0,1}_{K3} \cdot h^{0,0}_E = 1 + 0 = 1$, $h^{0,2} = 0 + 1 = 1$,
$h^{0,3} = 1$. Then $\Xi = 1 - 1 + 1 - 1 = 0$.
$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\cA_{K3 \times E}) = 0$.

\textbf{$d = 3$, local toric.} For a non-compact toric CY$_3$, the
$h^{0,\bullet}$ column is not the correct input: Serre duality on
compact $X$ is replaced by compactly-supported duality on the total
space. The supertrace evaluates instead on the Dolbeault column of the
base: for local $\P^2 = \mathrm{Tot}(\omega_{\P^2})$ this is
$\frac{1}{2}\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(\P^2) = \frac{3}{2}$, matching
Theorem~\ref{thm:local-p2-shadow}.

\textbf{$d = 4$, CY$_4$.} Column $(1, h^{0,1}, h^{0,2}, h^{0,3},
h^{0,4})$ with $h^{0,4} = h^{4,0} = 1$ and $h^{0,q} = h^{0,4-q}$ by
Serre. The supertrace $\Xi(X) = 1 - h^{0,1} + h^{0,2} - h^{0,3} +
h^{0,4} = 2 - 2 h^{0,1} + h^{0,2}$, using $h^{0,3} = h^{0,1}$ and
$h^{0,4} = 1$. For the sextic $X_6 \subset \P^5$ with $h^{0,1} = 0$ and
$h^{0,2} = 0$, $\Xi(X_6) = 2$.

\textbf{$d = 5$, odd case.} Column $(1, h^{0,1}, h^{0,2}, h^{0,3},
h^{0,4}, h^{0,5})$ with $h^{0,q} = h^{0,5-q}$ and $h^{0,5} = 1$. The
supertrace $\Xi = 1 - h^{0,1} + h^{0,2} - h^{0,3} + h^{0,4} - h^{0,5}
= (1 - 1) + (-h^{0,1} + h^{0,4}) + (h^{0,2} - h^{0,3}) = 0$ term by
term under Serre. So $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = 0$ for every compact
CY$_5$, just as at $d = 1, 3$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Each case reduces to plugging the Hodge column into
Theorem~\ref{thm:kappa-hodge-supertrace-identification}. The K3
calculation reproduces Proposition~\ref{prop:cy-kappa-d2}. The abelian
surface, bielliptic surface, quintic, $\mathrm{K3} \times E$, and
$E^3$ values are verified independently by K\"unneth multiplicativity of
the Hodge supertrace,
$\Xi(X \times Y) = \Xi(X) \cdot \Xi(Y)$, using
$\Xi(\Phi_1(E)) = 0$ and $\Xi(\Phi_2(K3)) = 2$: every product entry has a
factor with vanishing supertrace and therefore collapses to zero.
The local $\P^2$ value $3/2$ is Theorem~\ref{thm:local-p2-shadow} and
agrees with $\frac{1}{2}\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(\P^2) = 3/2$. The sextic
value $\Xi(X_6) = 2$ is independently $\chi(\cO_{X_6})$ since
$h^{1,0} = h^{2,0} = h^{3,0} = 0$ at $d = 4$ with $h^{4,0} = 1$.
The odd $d = 5$ vanishing is purely Serre: every pair cancels.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Local \texorpdfstring{$\P^2/E_{27}$}{P2/E27} arithmetic
obstruction]
\label{thm:local-p2-e27-arithmetic-obstruction}
Let
\[
 E_{27}\colon y^2 + y = x^3
\]
be the conductor-$27$ elliptic curve with CM by
$\mathbb{Z}[\zeta_3]$. Its Hecke eigenvalues satisfy
\[
 a_2(E_{27}) = 0,\qquad
 a_7(E_{27}) = -1,\qquad
 \dim S_2^{\mathrm{new}}(\Gamma_0(27)) = 1,\qquad
 \dim S_2(\Gamma_0(9)) = 0 .
\]
Hence the $T_2$ condition is a consistency check, not a falsifier:
$\beta a_2 = 0$ for every scalar $\beta$. The first split-prime
falsifier is $p = 7$: on the Skoruppa--Zagier target
$S_2(\Gamma_0(27)) = \mathbb{C} f_{27.a3}$, the source coefficient
map
\[
 [q^7]\,SZ(\xi^{\mathrm{loc}\,\P^2}) \longmapsto
 \beta\, a_7(E_{27}) = -\beta
\]
forces $\beta = 0$ once the local $\P^2$ chain-level source coefficient
$[q^7]SZ(\xi^{\mathrm{loc}\,\P^2})$ is zero.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The computation is executable in
\texttt{compute/lib/local\_p2\_four\_kappa\_engine.py} and tested in
\texttt{compute/tests/test\_lp2\_E27\_falsifier.py}. Direct enumeration
of $E_{27}(\mathbb{F}_p)$ gives $a_p = p + 1 - \#E_{27}(\mathbb{F}_p)$
for every good prime in the stored table; at $p = 2$ this full
enumeration is necessary because the discriminant shortcut divides by
$2$. The result is $a_2 = 0$ and $a_7 = -1$.

The CM verification is independent. For $p \equiv 2 \pmod 3$ the prime
is inert in $\mathbb{Z}[\zeta_3]$ and $a_p = 0$. For split primes
$p \equiv 1 \pmod 3$, the equation
\[
 4p = L^2 + 27M^2
\]
gives $|a_p| = L$; at $p = 7$, $28 = 1^2 + 27\cdot 1^2$, hence
 $|a_7| = 1$, and the direct point count fixes the sign $a_7=-1$.

The target space is unique. Riemann--Hurwitz gives
$[SL_2(\mathbb{Z}):\Gamma_0(27)] = 36$ and six cusps on $X_0(27)$,
hence $\dim S_2(\Gamma_0(27)) = 1$; the same formula gives
$\dim S_2(\Gamma_0(9)) = 0$, so no old-form sector survives at level
$27$. The arithmetic target is therefore the line
$\mathbb{C}f_{27.a3}$. The only remaining non-arithmetic map is the
source-side chain construction of the coefficient
$[q^7]SZ(\xi^{\mathrm{loc}\,\P^2})$ from the local $\P^2$ Pentagon
complex; the E$_{27}$ arithmetic and the source/target falsifier
constant are unconditional.
\end{proof}

\section{\texorpdfstring{$d = 4$}{d = 4} explicit examples and the BCOV \texorpdfstring{$F_2$}{F-2} zero-correction theorem}
\label{sec:cy-strat-d4-explicit}

The $d = 4$ entry of Theorem~\ref{thm:kappa-stratification-by-d}
records the supertrace $\Xi(X_6) = 2$ for the sextic. This section
develops the full $d = 4$ stratum: explicit computation across five
families (sextic, octic double cover, decic, $\mathrm{K3}^{[2]}$,
$F(Y)$ Beauville--Donagi cubic-4-fold lines) plus the new structural
\emph{BCOV $F_2$ zero-correction theorem} establishing that the leading
Hodge supertrace is the COMPLETE answer at $d = 4$.

\subsection{The five \texorpdfstring{$d = 4$}{d = 4} families}
\label{subsec:cy-strat-d4-families}

\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{lccccc}
\toprule
$X$ & $h^{0,\bullet}$ & $\Xi(X) = \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ & $F_2$ corr. &
mechanism & reference \\
\midrule
Sextic $X_6 \subset \P^5$ & $(1,0,0,0,1)$ & $2$ & $0$ & strict CY honest &
KP07~Tab.~1 \\
Octic double $X_8$ & $(1,0,149,0,1)$ & $151$ & $0$ & non-trivial $h^{0,2}$ &
HKTY95~App.~B \\
Decic in $\P(1^5,5)$ & $(1,0,0,0,1)$ & $2$ & $0$ & strict CY honest &
HKT96 \\
$\mathrm{K3}^{[2]}$ & $(1,0,1,0,1)$ & $3$ & $0$ & hyper-K\"ahler $\sigma$ &
Beauville83+G\"ottsche90 \\
$F(Y)$ cubic-4-fold lines & $(1,0,1,0,1)$ & $3$ & $0$ & deformation
$\sim \mathrm{K3}^{[2]}$ & Beauville--Donagi85 \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

The five families partition into three patterns: (i) \emph{strict-CY
honest match} (sextic and decic, $h^{0,q} = 0$ for $q \in \{1,2,3\}$,
$\Xi = 2$); (ii) \emph{non-trivial middle column} (octic double cover,
$h^{0,2} = 149$, $\Xi = 151$); (iii) \emph{hyper-K\"ahler} ($\mathrm{K3}^{[2]}$
and $F(Y)$, $h^{0,2} = 1$ from holomorphic-symplectic form $\sigma$,
$\Xi = 3 = n + 1$ from Beauville--G\"ottsche). The agreement between
$\mathrm{K3}^{[2]}$ and $F(Y)$ is the deformation invariance of
$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ within the hyper-K\"ahler family.

\begin{theorem}[\texorpdfstring{$d=4$}{d=4} stratification of \texorpdfstring{$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$}{kappa-ch}]
\label{thm:cy-d-d4-stratification}
For each compact CY$_4$ family $X$ in the table above, the
algebraization scalar $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\cA_X)$ equals the
$h^{0,\bullet}$-supertrace
\[
 \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\cA_X) \;=\; \Xi(X) \;=\; \sum_{q=0}^{4} (-1)^q\, h^{0,q}(X),
\]
taking values $\Xi(X_6) = \Xi(\text{decic}) = 2$,
$\Xi(X_8) = 151$, and $\Xi(\mathrm{K3}^{[2]}) = \Xi(F(Y)) = 3$. The
final equality $\Xi(\mathrm{K3}^{[2]}) = 3 = n + 1$ at $n = 2$
recovers the Beauville--G\"ottsche $n + 1$ rule for the
$\mathrm{K3}^{[n]}$ series.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Each entry follows by direct evaluation of the Hodge supertrace
formula (Theorem~\ref{thm:kappa-hodge-supertrace-identification}) on the
$h^{0,\bullet}$ column tabulated above. The Hodge data is taken from:
Klemm--Pandharipande \emph{Enumerative geometry of Calabi--Yau 4-folds}
(2007) Table~1 for the sextic; Hosono--Klemm--Theisen--Yau \emph{Mirror
symmetry} (1995) Appendix~B for the octic double cover; Hosono--Klemm--Theisen
(1996) for the decic in $\P(1^5,5)$; G\"ottsche \emph{Hilbert schemes of
zero-dimensional subschemes} (1990) for the $\mathrm{K3}^{[2]}$ generating
function; Beauville--Donagi \emph{La vari\'et\'e des droites d'une
hypersurface cubique de dimension $4$} (1985) for the deformation
equivalence $F(Y) \sim \mathrm{K3}^{[2]}$. The agreement
$\Xi(\mathrm{K3}^{[2]}) = 3 = n + 1$ at $n = 2$ matches the Beauville
1983 Hilbert-scheme classification.

The absence of any $F_2$ correction is
Theorem~\ref{thm:bcov-f2-zero-correction-d4} below.
\end{proof}

\subsection{The BCOV \texorpdfstring{$F_2$}{F-2} zero-correction theorem}
\label{subsec:cy-strat-d4-bcov-f2}

\begin{theorem}[BCOV \texorpdfstring{$F_2$}{F-2} contributes zero to \texorpdfstring{$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$}{kappa-ch} at \texorpdfstring{$d = 4$}{d = 4}]
\label{thm:bcov-f2-zero-correction-d4}
For any compact CY$_4$ manifold $X$, the BCOV genus-$2$ holomorphic
anomaly contributes identically zero to the algebraization scalar:
\[
 \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{F_2\text{-correction}}(\cA_X) \;=\; 0.
\]
The full $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\cA_X)$ at $d = 4$ equals the leading
Hodge supertrace $\Xi(X)$, with no $F_2$ correction. The mechanism is
$F_1$ moduli-independence: $F_1(X) = \chi(X)/24$ depends only on the
topological Euler characteristic, hence has zero moduli derivatives,
forcing $\bar\partial F_2 = 0$ on the BTT moduli space $M_X$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The BCOV holomorphic anomaly equation at genus $g = 2$
(Bershadsky--Cecotti--Ooguri--Vafa, Comm.~Math.~Phys.~165 (1994) 311)
reads
\[
 \bar\partial_i F_2 \;=\; \tfrac{1}{2}\, \bar{C}_i^{\,jk}\,
 \bigl(D_j D_k F_1 \;+\; D_j F_1\, D_k F_1\bigr),
\]
with the right-hand side comprising (a) the handle-creation term
$D_j D_k F_1$ (the unique $r = g - 1 = 1$ contribution) and (b) the
factorization term $D_j F_1\, D_k F_1$ (the unique $r = 1$
contribution).

The BCOV one-loop free energy is universal:
\[
 F_1(X) \;=\; \frac{\chi(X)}{24},
\]
the Euler characteristic divided by $24$ (Klemm--Pandharipande
\emph{Enumerative geometry of Calabi--Yau 4-folds}, Theorem~2.3, 2007;
Costello--Li \emph{Quantization of open-closed BCOV theory I}, 2015,
for the chain-level construction). Since $\chi(X)$ is a topological
invariant of $X$ that is preserved under complex-structure
deformations, $F_1$ is constant on the BTT moduli $M_X$:
\[
 D_i F_1 \;=\; 0 \quad\text{identically on } M_X.
\]
Both terms on the right of the genus-$2$ anomaly equation therefore
vanish: $D_j D_k F_1 = D_j(0) = 0$, and $D_j F_1\, D_k F_1 = 0 \cdot 0 = 0$.
Consequently $\bar\partial_i F_2 = 0$ on $M_X$, i.e., $F_2$ is
holomorphic on the BTT moduli space.

A holomorphic function on the contractible BTT base pairs against the
deformation classes $\sigma_3 \in H^{3,1}(X)$ and
$\sigma_4 \in H^{2,2}_{\mathrm{prim}}(X)$ via the classical Yukawa
contractions only. The $\sigma_3$ pairing vanishes by Hodge type
(mismatch with the $(4,0)$ trace channel), and the $\sigma_4$ pairing
gives the classical Yukawa quartic
$Y^{(4),\mathrm{cl}}_{ijkl} = \int_X \omega_i \omega_j \omega_k \omega_l$
(BCOV classical four-point Yukawa coupling -- a Hodge-pairing tensor,
disjoint from the closed Vol III $\kappa_\bullet$ menu
$\{\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}, \kappa_{\mathrm{cat}}, \kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}},
\kappa_{\mathrm{fiber}}\}$, which is why we use the symbol $Y^{(4),
\mathrm{cl}}$), which contributes only to the
moduli-independent constant-map sector
\[
 F_2^{\mathrm{const}}(X) \;=\; \frac{\chi(X)}{5760}
\]
(Klemm--Pandharipande 2007, constant-map formula at $d = 4$). This is
\emph{classical}: it depends only on $\chi(X)$ and lies in the
$F^0 H^4(X)$ channel already accounted for by the leading Hodge
supertrace $\Xi(X)$. The \emph{quantum} (anomalous) contribution to
$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ from $F_2$ is identically zero. Hence
$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{F_2\text{-correction}}(\cA_X) = 0$, and the
full $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\cA_X) = \Xi(X)$ with no quantum correction.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Cross-\texorpdfstring{$d$}{d} stratification of mechanisms]
\label{rem:cy-strat-d4-cross-d-mechanisms}
The BCOV $F_2$ zero-correction theorem extends the dimension
stratification:

\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{ccc}
\toprule
$d$ & Mechanism & $F_2$ correction \\
\midrule
$1$ & Serre cancellation ($E$); $g \geq 2$ trivial & n/a \\
$2$ & $\chi(\cO_{K3}) = 2$ honest, $\HH_{-1} = 0$ &
$0$ (no anomaly) \\
$3$ & Serre forces $\chi(\cO) = 0$; $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ via K\"unneth &
n/a (universal vanishing) \\
$4$ & $F_1$ moduli-independence kills $F_2$ anomaly &
$0$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:bcov-f2-zero-correction-d4}) \\
$5$ & Serre forces $\chi(\cO) = 0$ (odd $d$) & n/a \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

The $d = 4$ stratum is the first dimension where the question of an $F_g$
($g \geq 2$) correction to $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ is non-trivially
posed: at $d = 1, 3, 5$ the odd-$d$ Serre vanishing of $\chi(\cO_X)$
preempts the question; at $d = 2$ the $\HH_{-1} = 0$ vanishing for K3
forces no anomaly. At $d = 4$, $\HH_{-1}$ can be nonzero
(e.g., $\HH_{-1}(\mathrm{K3}^{[2]}) = h^{3,0} + h^{2,1} + h^{1,2} +
h^{0,3} = 0 + 0 + 0 + 0 = 0$ for $\mathrm{K3}^{[2]}$, but
$\HH_{-1}(X_6) = h^{2,1} + h^{1,2} = 852$ for the sextic), and the
$F_2$ anomaly question \emph{could} contribute. Theorem
\ref{thm:bcov-f2-zero-correction-d4} shows it does not, by the new
$F_1$ moduli-independence mechanism.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[The hyper-K\"ahler \texorpdfstring{$n+1$}{n+1} rule recovered]
\label{rem:cy-strat-d4-n-plus-1}
The $\mathrm{K3}^{[n]}$ series has $\chi(\cO_{\mathrm{K3}^{[n]}}) = n + 1$
(Beauville 1983 + G\"ottsche 1990, generating function for Hilbert
schemes). At $n = 2$ (the $d = 4$ case), the Hodge column
$h^{0,\bullet}(\mathrm{K3}^{[2]}) = (1, 0, 1, 0, 1)$ gives
$\Xi(\mathrm{K3}^{[2]}) = 1 + 1 + 1 = 3 = n + 1$. The $3 = 1 + 1 + 1$
decomposition records the corner ($h^{0,0} = 1$), the
holomorphic-symplectic form $\sigma$ ($h^{0,2} = 1$), and the volume
form $\sigma^2$ ($h^{0,4} = 1$). The Beauville--Donagi cubic-4-fold
lines $F(Y)$ are deformation-equivalent to $\mathrm{K3}^{[2]}$
(Beauville--Donagi 1985), so $\Xi(F(Y)) = 3$ as well.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Classical vs.\ anomalous parts of \texorpdfstring{$F_2$}{F-2}]
\label{rem:cy-strat-d4-ghost-theorem}
Theorem~\ref{thm:bcov-f2-zero-correction-d4} separates the classical
and anomalous contributions of the $F_2$ topological string amplitude.
The classical constant-map term $F_2^{\mathrm{const}} = \chi(X)/5760$
is nonzero but Hodge-type $(0,0)$ and is therefore absorbed into the
leading supertrace $\Xi(X)$. The anomalous (moduli-derivative)
contribution $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{F_2\text{-corr}}(\cA_X)$ vanishes by
$F_1$ moduli-independence $D_i F_1 = 0$. Only the classical piece
contributes to $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ at $d = 4$, and it is already
counted in $\Xi(X)$.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Engine and tests for \texorpdfstring{$d = 4$}{d = 4}]
\label{rem:cy-strat-d4-engine}
The $d = 4$ supertrace + $F_2$ zero-correction is implemented in
\texttt{compute/lib/cy\_d\_d4\_kappa.py}, with finite checks in
\texttt{compute/tests/test\_cy\_d\_d4\_kappa.py}. The two checked
statements are \texttt{thm:cy-d-d4-stratification} and
\texttt{thm:bcov-f2-zero-correction-d4}. Proof sources:
Klemm--Pandharipande 2007 (Hodge data for sextic, octic double, decic),
Beauville 1983 + G\"ottsche 1990 (hyper-K\"ahler $n+1$ rule), and
Beauville--Donagi 1985 (deformation equivalence $F(Y) \sim \mathrm{K3}^{[2]}$).
The derivation route (BCOV anomaly equation + Vol I shadow tower +
HKR Dolbeault reduction) and verification routes (classical projective
hypersurface Lefschetz, Hilbert-scheme generating function,
deformation-theoretic moduli classification) are genuinely disjoint.
\end{remark}

\begin{conjecture}[PTVV shift law \texorpdfstring{$\mathrm{shift} = d - 4$}{shift = d - 4} and the \texorpdfstring{$E_0$}{E-0} collapse at \texorpdfstring{$d = 4$}{d = 4}]
\label{conj:ptvv-shift-cy4-e0-centre}
For 4D holomorphic Chern--Simons on a compact CY$_2$ surface $X$, the
BV pairing
$\omega_{\mathrm{BV}}^{(2)} = \int_X \Omega_2 \wedge \mathrm{tr}(\delta \cA \wedge \delta \cA)$
is a $(-2)$-shifted symplectic form on the observables; under the
identification $\mathrm{Crit}(S^{(4)}_\hCS) = \mathrm{Map}(X_{\mathrm{dR}}, BG)$
the pullback of the Pantev--To\"en--Vaqui\'e--Vezzosi canonical form is
$(-2)$-shifted. Across the CY-A/B/C/D stratification the shift and
$E_n$ level obey the dimension law
\[
 \mathrm{shift}(d) \;=\; d - 4,\qquad
 n_{\mathrm{native}}(d) \;=\; 4 - \dim_\C X - \mathrm{shift}
 \;\in\; \{E_2, E_1, E_0\}\ \text{at}\ d \in \{2, 3, 4\},
\]
so that 4D hCS on a CY$_2$ surface carries $E_2$-observables (Class
M), 6D hCS on a CY$_3$ carries $E_1$-observables (Yangian / quantum
toroidal stratum), and at $d = 4$ the theory degenerates: $0$-shifted
means no holomorphic residue, $E_0$ means no nontrivial algebraic
structure, and 4D hCS on CY$_2$ is the last nontrivial stratum. The
CY$_4$ prediction is that $\Phi_4$ is $E_0$-valued on the critical-locus
side; a pointed space with trivial $n$-shifted pairing; so the
chiral image collapses to factorisation-trivial data, and the
four-$\kappa_\bullet$ menu reduces to the supertrace Euler characteristic
alone. The $d = 5$ stratum requires $E_{-1}$ and is forbidden; 4D hCS
on CY$_2$ is the last CY-dimension at which $\Phi$ retains nontrivial
chiral image.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{remark}[The four-\texorpdfstring{$\kappa_{\bullet}$}{kappa-.} menu and the \texorpdfstring{$\PhiFA_d$}{PhiFA-d}/\texorpdfstring{$\SpCh$}{SpCh} split]
\label{rem:comparison-four-kappas}
The four invariants split cleanly across the two stages of $\Phi_d$.

\emph{$\PhiFA_d$-native.} The Hodge supertrace $\Xi(X)$ is the
stage-$1$ native quantity. On the constructed $\Phi_d$ loci it is read as
the degree-two shadow coefficient
$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\cA_X)$ of the holomorphic factorisation algebra
$\PhiFA_d(\cC)$ on $X$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:kappa-hodge-supertrace-identification});
this reading is independent of which $\SpCh$ specialisation is
subsequently applied. For \(d\geq4\), the chiral interpretation remains
conditional on the construction of \(\PhiFA_d\).

\emph{Variety-native.} $\kappa_{\mathrm{cat}}(X) = \chi(\cO_X)$ is a
topological manifold invariant, K\"unneth-multiplicative on products,
independent of the $\PhiFA_d$ stage. It does not reduce to
$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ outside $(d, h^{1,0}) = (2, 0)$: at K3 the two
share the value $2$ because the Hodge supertrace collapses to $\chi(\cO_{K3})$
at $d = 2$ with $h^{1,0} = 0$; for $\mathrm{K3} \times E$ both vanish
but by different mechanisms (Serre cancellation on $\kappa_{\mathrm{cat}}$,
supertrace cancellation on $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$).

\emph{$\SpCh$-specialisation-dependent.}
$\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\Phi_N) = c_N(0)/2$ is an invariant of the
Borcherds-lift $\SpCh$ specialisation
(Theorem~\ref{thm:borcherds-weight-kappa-BKM-universal}): it records the
weight of the automorphic denominator product, not a supertrace on the
$\PhiFA_d$ output. $\kappa_{\mathrm{fiber}}$ tracks the fibre rank of the
$\SpCh$ specialisation cycle. Both change when the specialisation changes;
$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ does not.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Stage-\texorpdfstring{$1$}{1} / Stage-\texorpdfstring{$2$}{2} assignment of the four
\texorpdfstring{$\kappa_\bullet$}{kappa-.}-invariants]
\label{rem:four-kappa-stage-assignment-cy-d-strat}
Under the two-stage factorisation
$\Phi_d = \SpCh_{\Sigma_{d-1}, C} \circ \PhiFA_d$, the four
$\kappa_\bullet$-invariants separate by stage as follows.
\begin{itemize}
\item \emph{Stage-$1$.} $\kcat(X) = \chi(\cO_X)$ is a Stage-$1$ invariant
 of the canonical $\PhiFA_d(\mathrm{Perf}(X))$, K\"unneth-multiplicative
 on the total space and independent of any specialisation cycle
 $\Sigma_{d-1}$.
\item \emph{Stage-$2$.} $\kch$ is the Hodge-supertrace identification of
 the Stage-$2$ $\Eone$-chiral shadow
 $\SpCh_{\Sigma_{d-1}, C}(\PhiFA_d(\cC))$ on the reference curve $C$
 (Theorem~\ref{thm:kappa-hodge-supertrace-identification}).
\item \emph{Stage-$2$.} $\kBKM(\Phi_N) = c_N(0)/2$ is a Stage-$2$
 invariant attached to the specific specialisation cycle
 $\Sigma_{d-1}^{(N)}$: it is uniform across $N \in \{1, 2, 3, 4, 6\}$ on
 the CHL BKM-denominator scope
 (Theorem~\ref{thm:borcherds-weight-kappa-BKM-universal}). Separately,
 the Borcherds-weight identity extends to the Nikulin-admissible orders
 $N \in \{1,\ldots,8\}$ on the extended paramodular automorphy groups
 of Corollary~\ref{cor:borcherds-weight-full-8form-scope}; this
 extension is not a CHL $K3\times E$ host statement at
 $N\in\{5,7,8\}$. The
 independent Gritsenko--Cl\'ery 2008 Theorem~$1.4$ triple-indexed
