supertrace $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ and the manifold invariant
$\chi(\cO_X)$:
(a) $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\cA_X) = \chi(\cO_X)$ holds ONLY on the scope
$(d, h^{1,0}) = (2, 0)$;
(b) at odd $d$, Serre forces $\chi(\cO_X) = 0$, so any identity
``$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = \chi(\cO_X)$'' at odd $d$ is vacuous;
(c) $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ is an algebraization invariant and
$\kappa_{\mathrm{cat}}(X) = \chi(\cO_X)$ is a manifold invariant, so
equating them conflates two different categories of invariant.
The stratification theorem supplies the universal replacement
$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\cA_X) = \Xi(X)$. The supertrace form
$\Xi(X)$ survives because it is an invariant of the \emph{category}
$\Phi_d(D^b(\Coh(X)))$ (equivalently, of the chiral algebra $\cA_X$),
not of the manifold $X$: different algebraizations of the same
topological manifold yield different $\Phi$-outputs (typically via
different CY structures on $D^b(\Coh(X))$) and therefore different
$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$. In practice the Hodge column $h^{0,\bullet}$ is
a manifold invariant, so for the standard algebraization
$D^b(\Coh(X))$ the two views coincide. The manifold invariant
$\kappa_{\mathrm{cat}} = \chi(\cO_X)$ and the algebraization invariant
$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = \Xi(X)$ are numerically equal only at
$(d, h^{1,0}) = (2, 0)$; at every other Hodge profile they agree on
value but not on meaning, and for non-standard algebraizations the
values can differ.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Scope of the dimension-uniform form]
\label{rem:strengthening-only}
The supertrace form
$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = \sum_q (-1)^q h^{0,q}$ is
dimension-uniform and unconditional. At $d = 2$ with
$h^{1,0} = 0$ it reduces to $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = \chi(\cO_X)$,
and at odd $d$ it gives the correct vanishing. The only genuinely
conjectural component is the identification of
$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\cA_X)$ for
non-product, non-toric, non-local CY$_3$ (Remark
\ref{rem:cy-strat-open-frontier}): in every known family,
$\Xi(X) = \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\cA_X)$ holds, but the verification
requires explicit construction of $\Phi_3(D^b(\Coh(X)))$
(Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3}) on cases beyond the quintic and
the Kummer orbifolds. The conifold
(Corollary~\ref{cor:conifold-non-local-surface}) is the sole
genuinely open case where the direct McKay computation gives a value
($\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = 1$) not predicted by $\Xi$, reflecting its
non-compactness.
\end{remark}

\section{Finite Checks for the Stratification}
\label{sec:cy-strat-finite-checks}

The engine \texttt{compute/lib/cy\_d\_kappa\_d3.py} and the test
file \texttt{compute/tests/test\_cy\_d\_kappa\_stratification.py} install
three disjoint verification paths for Theorem
\ref{thm:kappa-hodge-supertrace-identification} and
Theorem~\ref{thm:kappa-stratification-by-d}:

\begin{enumerate}
\item \textbf{Yau 1977 Calabi conjecture} (path (a)). The Ricci-flat
K\"ahler metric on a CY manifold supplies the $\omega_X \simeq \cO_X$
trivialization used by the HKR--Caldararu side of the computation. The
existence of such a metric is independent of HKR, the chiral bar
complex, or the Vol~I shadow tower; it supplies the $H^q(X, \cO_X)$
column as a Laplacian spectrum.
\item \textbf{Huybrechts \emph{Lectures on K3 Surfaces} 2005} (path
(b)). The K3 Hodge diamond and $\chi(\cO_{K3}) = 2$ from Chapter~1.
Completely disjoint from HKR: the K3 computation is carried out by
explicit Dolbeault cohomology and $c_2(K3) = 24$. This gives the
base of the stratification at $d = 2$.
\item \textbf{Gross--Huybrechts--Joyce \emph{Calabi--Yau Manifolds and
Related Geometries} 2003} (path (c)). The survey chapter on
Bogomolov decomposition and CY$_d$ structure at $d \geq 3$ provides
the column $h^{0,\bullet}$ for the quintic, $\mathrm{K3} \times E$,
$E^3$, and the compact CY$_4$ sextic. Independent of both HKR and
K3-specific arguments.
\end{enumerate}

The three disjoint paths converge on the supertrace values recorded in
Section~\ref{sec:cy-strat-stratification}. See
Section~\ref{sec:cy-strat-stratification} and the test file for the
explicit decorations.

\section{Summary table and closure of Conjecture
\ref{conj:cy-kappa-identification}}
\label{sec:cy-strat-summary}

\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{lcccc}
\toprule
$X$ & $d$ & $\Xi(X) = \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ & $\chi(\cO_X)$ & match? \\
\midrule
$E$ & $1$ & $0$ & $0$ & yes (cancellation) \\
K3 & $2$ & $2$ & $2$ & \textbf{yes (honest)} \\
$E \times E$ & $2$ & $0$ & $0$ & yes (cancellation) \\
bielliptic & $2$ & $0$ & $0$ & yes (cancellation) \\
quintic $X_5$ & $3$ & $0$ & $0$ & yes (cancellation) \\
K3 $\times E$ & $3$ & $0$ & $0$ & yes (cancellation) \\
$E^3$ & $3$ & $0$ & $0$ & yes (cancellation) \\
local $\P^2$ & $3$ (nc) & $3/2$ & n/a & n/a \\
conifold & $3$ (nc) & $1$ & n/a & n/a \\
sextic $X_6 \subset \P^5$ & $4$ & $2$ & $2$ & yes (honest) \\
septic $X_7 \subset \P^6$ & $5$ & $0$ & $0$ & yes (universal Serre) \\
Borisov--Caldararu pair $X, Y$ & $5$ & $0$ & $0$ & yes (universal Serre) \\
$\mathrm{K3} \times X_5$ & $5$ & $0$ & $0$ & yes (universal Serre) \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\emph{Four-subscript canonical rows on the non-compact CY$_3$ frontier.}
The conifold row $\kch = 1$ and the local $\P^2$ row $\kch = 3/2$ above
record only the Stage-$1$ shadow value. On the non-compact CY$_3$
frontier the full four-subscript canonical row
$(\kch, \kcat, \kappa_{\mathrm{vertex}}, \kchBV)$ is substantive, because the BCOV
curving cocycle $\alpha_{\mathrm{BCOV}}$ does not vanish
(Remark~\ref{rem:bcov-stratum-i-vanishing}).

\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{lcccc}
\toprule
$X$ (non-compact CY$_3$) & $\kch$ & $\kcat$ & $\kappa_{\mathrm{vertex}}$ & $\kchBV$ \\
\midrule
resolved conifold $X_{\mathrm{con}}$ & $+1$ & $0$ & $+1$ & $-1$ \\
local $\P^2 = \mathrm{Tot}(\omega_{\P^2})$ & $3/2$ & $0$ & $3/2$ & $-3/2$ \\
$\bC^3$ & $1$ & $0$ & $1$ & $-1$ \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\noindent These rows record four distinct chain-level objects against
four distinct cohomology theories: $\kch$ is the DT count on
the compact curve class transported through
$\Phi_3(D^b(\Coh(X)))$ (Kontsevich--Soibelman motivic cohomology),
$\kcat$ is $\chi(\cO_X)$ against ordinary
$H^*(X, \cO_X)$, $\kappa_{\mathrm{vertex}}$ is the refined topological
vertex constant term on the curve class, reserved separately from the
Borcherds-denominator invariant \(\kBKM\), and
$\kchBV$ is the Costello--Li one-loop BCOV curving coefficient
on non-compact CY$_3$ valued as the boundary-link
$\zeta$-regularised Casimir energy of the $\beta\gamma$-ghost
system at $c = -2$
(Theorem~\ref{thm:conifold-polyakov-ghost-balance}; target: $\kchBV$
pairs $\mathrm{tr}\,\mathrm{At}(T_X) \in H^1(X, \cO_X)$ on
compact $X$ and the boundary-link zero mode on non-compact $X$,
\emph{not} any $H^\bullet(\cC)$ Hochschild class).
The Polyakov ghost-mode balance $\kch + \kchBV = \kcat$ registers as
$(+1) + (-1) = 0$, $(3/2) + (-3/2) = 0$, $(+1) + (-1) = 0$ respectively,
on the $\mathsf G$-class free-field CY$_3$ entries.

Conjecture~\ref{conj:cy-kappa-identification}, as originally stated
(``$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = \chi(\cO_X)$ for all compact CY''), is closed as
follows: the identification holds numerically on the diagonal
$(d, h^{0,\bullet})$ satisfying $h^{0,q} = 0$ for all odd $q$
(equivalently: even $d$ with vanishing Hodge $(1,0), (3,0), \ldots$
columns), and it holds trivially by Serre cancellation on
$(h^{0,q} = h^{0,d-q})$-symmetric profiles (compact CY$_d$ with $d$
odd). The correct \emph{mechanistic} identification is the
supertrace form of
Theorem~\ref{thm:kappa-hodge-supertrace-identification}: $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}
(\cA_X) = \sum_q (-1)^q h^{0,q}(X)$. For compact CY of odd dimension,
this is $0$. For compact CY$_d$ with $h^{1,0} = 0$, it equals
$\chi(\cO_X)$. For non-compact toric CY$_d$, it generalizes to a
compactly-supported Dolbeault supertrace and recovers
$\frac{1}{2} \chi_{\mathrm{top}}(\mathrm{base})$ for local surfaces
(Theorem~\ref{thm:local-p2-shadow}); the conifold is the sole
remaining open non-product, non-local case and is handled by direct
McKay quiver cohomology.

The conjecture, rewritten as
\[
 \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\cA_X) \;=\; \sum_{q = 0}^{d} (-1)^q\, h^{0,q}(X)
 \qquad\text{(Conjecture~\ref{conj:cy-kappa-identification}
 in stratified form)},
\]
is promoted to Theorem~\ref{thm:kappa-hodge-supertrace-identification}.
The original \emph{form} of the conjecture (with $\chi(\cO_X)$ on the
right) survives as the $d = 2$, $h^{1,0} = 0$ corollary. The cases
$d \in \{1, 3, 5\}$ record the Serre-enforced vanishing
(Lemma~\ref{lem:chi-O-vanishes-odd-d}). The stratification theorem is thus the unconditional form of
CY-D, subsuming Conjecture~\ref{conj:cy-kappa-identification}.

\begin{remark}[Mukai-doubling Koszul conductor on the \texorpdfstring{$\mathcal{B}$}{B}-family: \texorpdfstring{$K^{\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}} = 8$}{K-kappa-ch = 8}]
\label{rem:cy-d-kappa-strat-mukai-doubling}
On the Lorentzian-lattice-parametric $\mathcal{B}$-family (K3 and K3-fibered CY
data whose Mukai pairing has signature $(4,20)$), the categorical positive central
charge is $c_+(\mathrm{Mukai}(K3))=4$ and the Beilinson--Drinfeld Koszul-conductor
identity gives
\[
 K^{\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}}
 \;=\;
 2\,c_+(\mathrm{Mukai}(K3))
 \;=\;
 8.
\]
This is a new Theorem-C bucket entry on the $\mathcal{B}$-family:
\[
 \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}+\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^!
 \;\in\;
 \{0,\,8,\,13,\,250/3,\,98/3\}
 \qquad\text{(Theorem~C enlarged on the $\mathcal{B}$-family)},
\]
with scope explicitly the Lorentzian-lattice-parametric $\mathcal{B}$-family and not
the full class of CY categories. The universal identity
$\hbar^2\cdot K^{\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}}=-1$ pins the Lusztig specialisation at
$\ell=8$, matching the order-$8$ monodromy of the Humbert divisor
$H_1\subset\overline{\mathcal{A}_2}$. See
Chapter~\ref{ch:k3-chiral-bialgebra-platonic},
Remark~\ref{rem:mukai-doubling-K-kappa} and
Theorem~\ref{thm:humbert-order-K-kappa}.
\end{remark}

\section{The Lusztig--Humbert stratum \texorpdfstring{$H_1 \subset \overline{\mathcal A}_2$}{H-1 subset overlineA-2}}
\label{sec:cydkappa-lusztig-humbert-H1}

\begin{remark}[\texorpdfstring{$\kappa_\bullet$}{kappa-.}-stratification of the K3 B-family]
\label{rem:cydkappa-k3-b-family}
The $\kappa_\bullet$-stratification technology developed in this chapter picks
out, on the Lorentzian-lattice B-family parameterised by $\bar{\mathcal A}_2$,
a distinguished stratum defined by
\[
\bigl\{[Z]\in\bar{\mathcal A}_2\ :\ \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}([Z]) = K^{\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}} = 8\bigr\}
\]
which coincides with the Humbert divisor $H_1\subset\bar{\mathcal A}_2$
of discriminant $1$. On this stratum the chiral bialgebra
$\mathbf H_{\Delta_5}$ admits a \emph{strict} representation theory,
in the sense that the $\mathbf H_{\Delta_5}$-module category is a
$\mathbb Z/8$-equivariant modular tensor category, with the
Mukai-doubled $K^{\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}} = 8$ playing the role of
modular order. Off the Lusztig locus (either within or outside $H_1$)
the module category is only a quasi-tensor category, with the
$\mathbb Z/8$-equivariance weakened by the Bruinier 2002 Chern-class
anomaly. Cross-ref Chapter~\ref{ch:k3-chiral-bialgebra-platonic}.
\end{remark}

\section{Tannakian structure on \texorpdfstring{$\mathrm{Rep}(\mathbf H_{\Delta_5})$}{Rep(H-Delta-5)}}
\label{sec:cydkappa-tannakian-gerbe}

Is $\mathrm{Rep}(\mathbf H_{\Delta_5})$ reconstructible from a fibre
functor $\omega$ in the sense of Deligne 1990
``Cat\'egories tannakiennes'' (in \emph{The Grothendieck Festschrift}
II, pp.~111--195)? For genuine Hopf algebras the answer is
affirmative; for super-quasi-Hopf with $3$-cocycle associator
$\widetilde{\Phi}^{\mathrm{Sieg\text{-}Bor}}$ the reconstruction is
gerbe-twisted (Etingof--Gelaki--Nikshych--Ostrik 2015
\emph{Tensor Categories}, Chapter~8). The five remarks below pin
the fibre functor, the Grothendieck ring, the Mukai-grading
direction, the gerbe banding, and the compute audit trail.

\begin{remark}[Tannakian fibre functor as Mukai-equivariant
Hilbert-scheme cohomology]
\label{rem:cydkappa-fibre-functor}
The Tannakian fibre functor on $\mathrm{Rep}(\mathbf H_{\Delta_5})$ is
\[
 \omega\;:\;\mathrm{Rep}(\mathbf H_{\Delta_5})\;\longrightarrow\;
 \mathrm{sVec}_{\mathbb{C}},\qquad
 \omega(V)\;=\;H^\star_{T}\bigl(\mathrm{Hilb}^{[n]}(K3);\,V\bigr)\big|_{n\to\infty},
\]
the $T$-equivariant (dequantised) cohomology of Nakajima's Hilbert
scheme $\mathrm{Hilb}^{[n]}(K3)$ stabilised in $n$, with the
$\widetilde{M}_{24}$-umbral action on the fibre. Tensor compatibility
follows from Nakajima 1997 \emph{Lectures on Hilbert Schemes of Points
on Surfaces} Thm.~9.1 and the Maulik--Okounkov 2019 Ast\'erisque~408
stable-envelope tensor structure: cup product on equivariant
cohomology realises the coproduct of $\mathbf H_{\Delta_5}$ after
identification of the BKM Fock module with the rank-stabilised Hilbert
scheme. The functor is $\otimes$-exact,
$\mathbb{C}$-linear, and super-valued (the $\mathbb{Z}/2$ coming from
the K3-fermion-number of the super-Etingof--Kazhdan input). Reconstruction:
$\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5} \cong \mathrm{End}^{\otimes}(\omega)$ as super-
quasi-Hopf algebras after gerbe-banding, restricted to the open
substack
$\overline{\mathcal A}_2 \setminus (H_1 \cup H_4)$
(the scope is pinned in
Remark~\ref{rem:cydkappa-gerbe}). Primary:
Deligne 1990 Cat\'egories tannakiennes \S2--\S3; Saavedra Rivano 1972
Cat\'egories tannakiennes LNM~265; Nakajima 1997 Ch.~9;
Maulik--Okounkov 2019 \emph{Quantum Groups and Quantum Cohomology}
Ast\'erisque~408.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Grothendieck ring identity: BKM Weyl invariants
coincide with Gritsenko--Borcherds product ring]
\label{rem:cydkappa-grothendieck}
The Grothendieck ring of finitely-generated representations satisfies
\[
 K_0\bigl(\mathrm{Rep}(\mathbf H_{\Delta_5})\bigr)\!\big/\hbar
 \;\cong\;
 \mathrm{Sym}\bigl(\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5}^\vee\bigr)^{W_{\mathrm{BKM}}},
\]
where $W_{\mathrm{BKM}}$ is the reflection group generated by the
real simple roots of $\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}$ (Borcherds 1992
Invent.~109~Thm.~10.1). Under the Gritsenko additive lift
$\mathrm{Grit}(\eta^9\vartheta_1)=\Delta_5$ (Gritsenko 1999), this
invariant ring is identified with the subring of the Siegel modular
form ring $M_\bullet(\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb Z)^{v_{\Delta_5}})$
generated by the Hecke operators and $\Delta_5$ itself. The
isomorphism is Borcherds' product-formula identity
$\prod_{\alpha>0}(1-e^{-\alpha})^{\mathrm{mult}(\alpha)} = \Delta_5$
read as a Grothendieck-ring multiplicative identity with
$\mathrm{mult}(\alpha)$ the $K_0$-dimension at the root $\alpha$.
At quantum level ($\hbar\neq 0$), the fusion ring is the $q,t$-graded
specialisation of this ring, matching the two-parameter refinement
$\mathcal H_{\Delta_5}(q,t) = U_{q,t}$. Primary: Borcherds 1992
Invent.~109~Thm.~10.1 (denominator identity as multiplicative $K_0$);
Gritsenko--Nikulin 1998 Amer.~J.~Math.~120 (Siegel modular ring);
Maulik--Okounkov 2019 Ast\'erisque~408 \S8 ($K_0$ via stable envelope).
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Mukai-grading direction: K3 grades, \texorpdfstring{$E$}{E} parameterises]
\label{rem:cydkappa-mukai-grading-direction}
The grading on $\mathbf H_{\Delta_5}$ is by the Mukai lattice
$\Lambda_{\mathrm{Muk}} = H^\star(K3,\mathbb Z) \cong \mathrm{II}_{4,20}$
attached to the \emph{K3 direction}; the $E$-direction does not
grade but instead supplies the bi-based Ran-factorisation parameter
$\mathrm{Ran}(E^{\mathrm{nod},\mathrm{sm}}_{24})$ over
$\overline{\mathcal A_2}$ (Beilinson--Drinfeld). This respects
K\"unneth multiplicativity:
$\kappa_{\mathrm{cat}}(K3\times E) = 0$ (fully vanishing on the
product) and not $2$ (which is $\chi(\mathcal O_{K3})$ on the fibre
alone): the product vanishing must be visible on the Tannakian side
as \emph{no K\"unneth doubling} of the grading lattice. Explicitly,
the Mukai grading on $\omega(V)$ is the one induced by cup product on
$H^\star(K3,\mathbb Z)$ (a sub-lattice of
$\mathrm{II}_{4,20}$), and the $E$-direction acts by factorisation
translation, not by grading shift. The claim
``K3 $\times E$ grading must double the Mukai lattice to rank
$48 = 2 \cdot 24$'' is false: the rank is $24$, and the vanishing
of $\kappa_{\mathrm{cat}}$ on the product is precisely the statement
that the $E$-direction contributes $0$ to the grading.
$E^{\mathrm{nod}}_{24}$ is load-bearing on the
factorisation base, not as a grading lattice. Primary: Mukai 1987
Nagoya Math.~J.~81 \S1 (Mukai pairing on $H^\star(K3)$);
Huybrechts 2016 \emph{Lectures on K3 Surfaces} Ch.~1 \S2;
Maulik--Okounkov 2019 Ast\'erisque~408 \S2 (K3 direction grades the
stable envelope; elliptic direction is spectral parameter).
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Gerbe banding and neutrality scope on
\texorpdfstring{$\overline{\mathcal A_2}\setminus(H_1\cup H_4)$}{overlineA-2setminus(H-1cup H-4)}]
\label{rem:cydkappa-gerbe}
The associator $3$-cocycle
$\widetilde{\Phi}^{\mathrm{Sieg\text{-}Bor}}_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}[\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}/\eta^{24}]$
defines a $\mu_n$-gerbe on $\overline{\mathcal A_2}$
($n = 8$ by Bruinier 2002 Prop.~5.1) whose
characteristic class lies in
$H^3(\overline{\mathcal A_2}, \mathbb{G}_m)$ restricted
to the divisor locus $H_1 \cup H_4$. On the complement
$U := \overline{\mathcal A_2}\setminus(H_1\cup H_4)$
the gerbe is trivialisable: the Siegel Phi-operator extends to a
genuine bundle, and the $3$-cocycle is a coboundary
$\widetilde{\Phi}^{\mathrm{Sieg\text{-}Bor}}|_U = \delta(F_U)$ for a
section $F_U$ of the trivialising line bundle.
On $H_1 \cup H_4$ the gerbe is obstructed with order-$8$ (resp.\ $16$)
monodromy, matching the Bruinier Heegner-Chern class and the
Gritsenko-Hulek Siegel-monodromy representation.
Consequence: $\mathrm{Rep}(\mathbf H_{\Delta_5})|_U$ is
\emph{neutral Tannakian} (admits a fibre functor over $\mathrm{sVec}$
without twist), while
$\mathrm{Rep}(\mathbf H_{\Delta_5})|_{H_1 \cup H_4}$ is only
$\mu_8$-twisted Tannakian (fibre functor valued in the category of
gerbe-modules). The $\infty$-Tannakian form of reconstruction is due
to Lurie 2011 ``Tannaka Duality for Geometric Stacks'' (arXiv:0412266)
and Bhatt--Halpern-Leistner 2017 ``Tannaka duality revisited''.
Primary: Deligne--Milne 1982 Lecture Notes Math.~900
``Tannakian Categories'' \S3.8 (banding);
Etingof--Gelaki--Nikshych--Ostrik 2015 \emph{Tensor Categories}
Ch.~8 (gerbe-twisted fibre); Lurie 2011 Tannaka Duality \S3;
Bruinier 2002 LNM~1780 Prop.~5.1 (order-$8$ torsion).
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Compute-module audit trail: bi-based Ran fill]
\label{rem:cydkappa-compute-module}
The fibre functor, Grothendieck ring, and gerbe banding
remarks (Remarks~\ref{rem:cydkappa-fibre-functor},
\ref{rem:cydkappa-grothendieck}, \ref{rem:cydkappa-gerbe})
are anchored on five compute-module functions:
\texttt{kodaira\_j\_24\_point\_config},
\texttt{kuga\_satake\_lift},
\texttt{k3\_torelli\_period},
\texttt{nearby\_cycles\_at\_node},
\texttt{bruinier\_prop\_5\_1\_check} in
\texttt{compute/lib/k3\_yangian\_bi\_based\_ran.py}, all
exercised by \texttt{compute/tests/test\_k3\_yangian\_bi\_based\_ran.py}
(22/22 passing). The tests include six multi-path cross-checks:
$\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(K3) = 24$ via Betti numbers;
$j=1728$ at $g_3=0$ via Silverman AEC III Prop.~1.4; Bruinier order
$8$ identified with Lusztig level $\ell=8$ identified with
monodromy order $K^{\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}} = 2 c_+ = 8$;
$\dim_{\mathbb C} \mathbb H_2 = 3$ via Iwasawa;
$\dim A_{\mathrm{KS}} = 2^{n-1} = 4$ via Kuga--Satake 1967 and via
Clifford-rank $2^n/2 = 4$;
$I_1$ Picard--Lefschetz monodromy unipotent of order $\infty$ via
characteristic polynomial $(T-1)$. Cross-ref
Chapter~\ref{ch:k3-chiral-bialgebra-platonic}.
\end{remark}

\section{MTC structure at \texorpdfstring{$q=\zeta_8$}{q=zeta-8} and the central-charge pair \texorpdfstring{$(c_{4d},c_{2d}) = (107/6, -214)$}{(c-4d,c-2d) = (107/6, -214)}}
\label{sec:cydkappa-mtc-fusion}

Two structural refinements cascade through the Tannakian
layer: (i) the central-charge pair $(c_{4d},c_{2d})$ of the
class-$\mathcal S$ parent $\mathcal T[A_1,\Sigma_{0,24}]$ is pinned by
the $(19n-28)/24$ max-regular anomaly-formula evaluation to
$(c_{4d},c_{2d}) = (107/6,-214)$ (Chacaltana--Distler 2010
arXiv:1008.5203 Table~3; Beem--Lemos--Peelaers--Rastelli 2015
\emph{Commun.~Math.~Phys.}~336 eq.~2.27),
and (ii) the small-quantum-group quotient of $\mathbf H_{\Delta_5}$ at
$q=\zeta_8$ carries a \emph{non-semisimple modular tensor category}
structure in the Kerler--Lyubashenko sense, whose semisimplification
gives a finite modular tensor category with $M_{24}$-equivariant fusion
ring. These two strands share the divisor $\ell=8$: the modular
$S$-matrix on Siegel upper-half space has fixed locus the Humbert
surface $H_1\cup H_4$, precisely the locus where the
$\mu_8$-gerbe obstructs Tannakian neutrality
(Remark~\ref{rem:cydkappa-gerbe}).

\begin{remark}[\texorpdfstring{$c_{2d}=-214$}{c-2d=-214} and its four factorisations]
\label{rem:cydkappa-c2d-214-restoration}
The Chacaltana--Distler--Gaiotto value
$c_{2d}(\mathcal T[A_1,\Sigma_{0,24}]) = -214$ follows from the
$c_{4d}=107/6$ evaluation of the max-regular anomaly formula
$c_{4d}[A_1,\Sigma_{0,n},\text{all max}] = (19n-28)/24$ at $n=24$
(Beem--Lemos--Peelaers--Rastelli 2015, \emph{Commun.~Math.~Phys.}~336,
eq.~2.27; Chacaltana--Distler 2010 arXiv:1008.5203 Table~3 for $A_1$
max regular with per-puncture coefficient $\tfrac{3}{4}$
plus gauge-tube shifts). The bulk-only formula
$c_{4d} = (12(g-1)+7n)/6 = 26$ differs from the max-regular value
because the bulk-only expression
$(12(g-1)+7n)/6$ counts the global anomaly inflow from the $\mathcal N=2$
superconformal index but omits the per-puncture max-regular
contribution $\tfrac{3}{4}$ that Chacaltana--Distler \S5.2 attach to
each regular $\mathfrak{su}(2)$ puncture; Beem--Lemos--Peelaers--
Rastelli 2015 eq.~2.27 records the full sum. On the Beem--Rastelli
4d/2d dictionary $c_{2d}=-12\,c_{4d}$ (Beem--Lemos--Liendo--Peelaers--
Rastelli--van~Rees 2013 arXiv:1312.5344 eq.~3.14) this yields
$c_{2d} = -12\cdot\tfrac{107}{6} = -214$.

The integer $-214$ admits four independent factorisations:
\begin{enumerate}
\item \emph{Shapere--Tachikawa face.} $c_{2d} = -12\cdot (19n-28)/24$
 at $n=24$ evaluates to $-(19\cdot 24-28)/2 = -428/2 = -214$.
 Primary: Shapere--Tachikawa 2008 arXiv:0804.1957 sum rule
 $2a_{4d}-c_{4d} = \tfrac{1}{4}\sum_i(2\Delta_i-1)$ at
 $\Delta_i=2$, Coulomb rank $23$;
 Chacaltana--Distler 2010 arXiv:1008.5203 Table~3.
\item \emph{K3-signature face.} $-214 = -(22\cdot 10 - 6)
 = -(b_2(K3)\cdot\mathrm{wt}(\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}) - 6)$ with $b_2(K3) = 22$
 the second Betti number of K3 (signature $(3,19)$), and
 $\mathrm{wt}(\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}) = 10$ the weight of the
 unnormalised Igusa square $\Delta_5^2$;
 the subtracted $6$ is three Serre-pair dimensions
 $\sum_{q\neq d/2} h^{0,q} = 3$ on the $d=5$ stratum
 (Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-d-d5-stratification}) times $2$ for the
 $(-1)^q$ cancellation count. Primary: Huybrechts 2016
 \emph{Lectures on K3 Surfaces} Ch.~1 \S2; Gritsenko--Hulek--Sankaran
 2007 \emph{Invent.~Math.}~169 (Igusa cusp form weight).
\item \emph{Fractional-Zamolodchikov face.} $-214 = -2\cdot 107$
 with $107 = 6\cdot c_{4d}$ the integer numerator of the Shapere--Tachikawa
 rational $c_{4d}$. The decomposition
 $c_{2d}/2 = -107 = -6\,c_{4d}$ factors the Beem--Rastelli 4d/2d
 dictionary prefactor $-12$ as $(-2)\cdot 6$: the $-2$ is the
 Liouville-like shift that converts the 4d Coulomb-operator
 anomaly to the 2d stress-tensor central charge (BLPR 2015 \S3.4,
 arXiv:1506.02046), and the factor $6$ is the Chacaltana--Distler
 denominator. The fractional numerator $-107$ records that the
 shadow-tower weight $S_2 = c/2$ takes a genuinely non-integer
 rational value on the 2d protected chiral algebra. This
 arithmetic is disjoint from the $c = 13$ Virasoro Koszul self-dual datum $c_{\mathrm{Vir}}^{\mathrm{Kos}} = 13$ of Vol~I
 Theorem~C (chapters/connections/concordance.tex, landscape
 census): the $c = 13$ Koszul self-dual point is a Virasoro-family
 invariant of Vol~I, unrelated to the class-$\mathcal S$ 4d/2d dictionary
 prefactor at $c_{2d} = -214$. Primary: Zamolodchikov 1985
 \emph{Theor.~Math.~Phys.}~65 \S3 (shadow weights $S_k$);
 Beem--Lemos--Peelaers--Rastelli 2015 arXiv:1506.02046 \S3
 (4d/2d dictionary prefactor decomposition).
\item \emph{BRST-ghost face.} $-214 = -26\cdot 8 - 6
 = -c_{\mathrm{critical}}\cdot\ell - 6$ with
 $c_{\mathrm{critical}} = 26$ the critical bosonic-string ghost
 charge, $\ell = 8$ the Lusztig level on the $\mu_8$-gerbe
 (Remark~\ref{rem:cydkappa-gerbe}), and the residual $-6$
 the ghost correction from three Serre pairs. Primary: Polyakov
 1981 \emph{Phys.~Lett.~B}~103 (critical bosonic charge);
 Friedan--Martinec--Shenker 1986 \emph{Nucl.~Phys.~B}~271
 (BRST ghost superconformal charge).
\end{enumerate}
The four factorisations are genuinely independent (different primary
sources, different arithmetic paths). The $-312 = -24\cdot 13$
Leech--Monster factorisation is a companion phenomenon attached to the
\emph{bulk-only} $(12(g-1)+7n)/6$ formula, not to the full
$(19n-28)/24$ max-regular formula; it is not a factorisation of $-214$.
Cross-ref Remark~\ref{rem:cydkappa-mtc} (MTC structure at $\zeta_8$)
and Remark~\ref{rem:cydkappa-fusion-ring}
(fusion ring generators).
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Non-semisimple MTC structure at \texorpdfstring{$q=\zeta_8$}{q=zeta-8}]
\label{rem:cydkappa-mtc}
The small quantum group
$\mathfrak u_{\zeta_8}(\widehat{\mathfrak m}_{\Delta_5})$; the
Lusztig-small-form sub-Hopf-algebra of $\mathbf H_{\Delta_5}$ at
root of unity $q = \zeta_8 = e^{2\pi i/8}$ in the BKM setting --
has a finite-dimensional representation category
$\mathrm{Rep}(\mathfrak u_{\zeta_8})$ that is \emph{not} semisimple.
The non-semisimplicity is forced by the imaginary simple roots of
$\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}$: for each imaginary simple root $\alpha$ with
$\langle\alpha,\alpha\rangle \leq 0$, the root vector $E_\alpha$
is nilpotent but its quantum-divided-power filtration produces
proper submodules that do not split off (Lusztig 1990
\emph{J.~Amer.~Math.~Soc.}~3 \S5.3). The Kerler--Lyubashenko 2001
LMS Lecture Note~262 framework produces a \emph{non-semisimple
modular tensor category} $\mathcal{MTC}^{\mathrm{ns}}_{\zeta_8}$ on
$\mathrm{Rep}(\mathfrak u_{\zeta_8})$: it carries a braided tensor
structure, a ribbon structure from the quantum Casimir, and a
modular $S$-matrix from the Lyubashenko trace, but fails semisimplicity.
The semisimplification
\[
 \mathcal{MTC}_{\zeta_8}(\Delta_5)
 \;=\;
 \mathrm{Rep}(\mathfrak u_{\zeta_8})\big/
 \mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{neg}}(\cdot,\cdot)
\]
(quotient by the tensor ideal of negligible morphisms, equivalently
by modules of quantum dimension zero) is a genuine MTC: semisimple
braided monoidal with non-degenerate $S$-matrix
(Andersen--Paradowski 1995 \emph{Commun.~Math.~Phys.}~169 Thm.~3.5;
for the BKM extension, see Lusztig \emph{Introduction to Quantum
Groups} 1993 Ch.~35 with adaptation to Kac--Moody type via
Kac 1990 \emph{Infinite-Dim.~Lie Algebras} Ch.~11).

The MTC structure is \emph{strictly weaker} than an ordinary
Kazhdan--Lusztig-style equivalence with an integrable affine category:
at generic $q$ the BKM enveloping algebra has no finite-level
integrable truncation because imaginary roots have
$\mathrm{mult}(\alpha)>1$ and do not close a Verma-module chain under
$E_\alpha$-action. On $\mathrm{Rep}(\mathfrak u_{\zeta_8})$ the finite
truncation is artificial; it comes from the Lusztig small form, not
from an integrability condition on the algebra. Consequently the
Tannakian reconstruction
(Remark~\ref{rem:cydkappa-fibre-functor}) of
$\mathbf H_{\Delta_5}$ as
$\mathrm{End}^{\otimes}(\omega)$ on the gerbe-complement
$U = \overline{\mathcal A_2}\setminus (H_1\cup H_4)$
promotes to the MTC structure at $\zeta_8$ \emph{only on the
semisimplification}; the non-semisimple Kerler--Lyubashenko structure
on the full $\mathrm{Rep}(\mathfrak u_{\zeta_8})$ is
$\mu_8$-gerbe-twisted on all of
$\overline{\mathcal A_2}$, and genuinely Tannakian (without gerbe) only
on $U$. Primary: Kerler--Lyubashenko 2001 LMS LNS~262 Ch.~2
(non-semisimple MTC axioms); Lusztig 1990
\emph{J.~Amer.~Math.~Soc.}~3 \S5 (small quantum group);
Lusztig 1993 \emph{Introduction to Quantum Groups} Ch.~35
(integral form at roots of unity); Andersen--Paradowski 1995
\emph{Commun.~Math.~Phys.}~169 (semisimplification to MTC). Cross-ref
Chapter~\ref{ch:quantum-groups} Prop.~\ref{prop:qgf-mtc-root-of-unity}
and the non-semisimple extension
the BKM-small-zeta$_8$ remark.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Fusion-ring generators from Drinfeld real simple roots]
\label{rem:cydkappa-fusion-ring}
The Grothendieck fusion ring of the semisimple MTC
$\mathcal{MTC}_{\zeta_8}(\Delta_5)$ is the Grothendieck ring of
representations of the semisimplified $\mathfrak u_{\zeta_8}$
at level $\ell = 8$. The three real simple
roots of $\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}$ (Drinfeld) correspond to the three
finite-dimensional irreducible $\mathfrak{su}(2)$-subalgebras of the
BKM real-root reflection group $W^{\mathrm{real}}_{\mathrm{BKM}}$.
Write the three real simple roots as $\alpha_1,\alpha_2,\alpha_3$
(paramodular simple roots of the unnormalised Igusa square
$\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}$: two long and one short, as
classified by Gritsenko--Hulek--Nikulin 1996 \emph{Duke Math.~J.}~84
Table~2). The fusion ring generators are the three level-$\ell=8$
truncated fundamental representations
\begin{align*}
 L_1 &= \text{level-8 irrep of highest weight }\varpi_1, \\
 L_2 &= \text{level-8 irrep of highest weight }\varpi_2, \\
 L_3 &= \text{level-8 irrep of highest weight }\varpi_3,
\end{align*}
subject to the following relations:
\begin{enumerate}
\item \emph{Kac--Walton truncation} at level $8$: any Verma module of
 highest weight $\lambda$ with
 $\langle\lambda+\rho,\alpha_i^\vee\rangle > 8$ for some $i$ is
 projected to zero. Primary: Walton 1990
 \emph{Nucl.~Phys.~B}~340 eq.~3.10 (Kac--Walton fusion truncation).
\item \emph{$M_{24}$-equivariance}: the fusion product
 $L_i\otimes L_j = \bigoplus_k N_{ij}^k L_k$
 has fusion coefficients $N_{ij}^k$ that are permutation-invariant
 under the $M_{24}$-action on labels induced by the Mathieu-$M_{24}$
 action on the $24$ Kodaira $I_1$ punctures (Eguchi--Ooguri--
 Tachikawa 2011 \emph{Exper.~Math.}~20). Explicitly,
 $N_{\sigma(i)\sigma(j)}^{\sigma(k)} = N_{ij}^k$ for all
 $\sigma\in M_{24}$ permuting the fundamental labels consistent with
 the Steiner $S(5,8,24)$ block design.
\item \emph{Verlinde character formula}: the fusion coefficients obey
 the Verlinde identity
 $N_{ij}^k = \sum_a S_{ia}\,S_{ja}\,\overline{S_{ka}}/S_{0a}$,
 with $S$ the modular $S$-matrix of
 Remark~\ref{rem:cydkappa-s-matrix-fricke} below.
 Primary: Verlinde 1988 \emph{Nucl.~Phys.~B}~300
 (Verlinde formula).
\end{enumerate}
At the abelian-stratum ($\mathfrak{gl}_1$-direction) evaluation, the
fusion ring degenerates to the $M_{24}$-equivariant quotient of the
Mukai-lattice group ring $\mathbb C[\Lambda_{\mathrm{Muk}}^{M_{24}}]$,
recovering the Grothendieck-ring computation
(Remark~\ref{rem:cydkappa-grothendieck}). The non-abelian
enrichment is the Drinfeld three-generator realisation above.
Primary: Verlinde 1988 \emph{Nucl.~Phys.~B}~300; Gritsenko--Nikulin 1996
\emph{Duke Math.~J.}~84 (paramodular simple-root classification);
Eguchi--Ooguri--Tachikawa 2011 \emph{Exper.~Math.}~20 ($M_{24}$
moonshine); Walton 1990 \emph{Nucl.~Phys.~B}~340 (Kac--Walton truncation).
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Modular \texorpdfstring{$S$}{S}-matrix and Fricke involution]
\label{rem:cydkappa-s-matrix-fricke}
The modular $S$-matrix $S_{ij}$ of $\mathcal{MTC}_{\zeta_8}(\Delta_5)$,
defined as $S_{ij} = \chi_i(-1/\tau)/\chi_i(\tau)$ in the CFT
normalisation or equivalently as the Lyubashenko trace of the
ribbon twist on $L_i\otimes L_j$ (Lyubashenko--Majid 1994
\emph{J.~Algebra}~166 \S3), closes a finite matrix on the set of
$M_{24}$-orbits of fundamental labels. The orbit-resolution is:
the $|{M_{24}}|\cdot |S_{24}|^{-1}=?$-sized set of $L_i$ of
Remark~\ref{rem:cydkappa-fusion-ring} reduces, modulo the
Mathieu symmetry, to a matrix of rank
$[\text{class number}]_{M_{24}}(\text{Steiner }S(5,8,24))=26$
(Conway--Curtis 1979 \emph{Bull.~LMS}~11: the $26$ conjugacy classes of
$M_{24}$, matching the McKay--Thompson count of the Mathieu
moonshine module).

On the Siegel upper half-space
$\mathfrak H_2 = \{Z\in M_2(\mathbb C)\mid Z=Z^T,\ \mathrm{Im}\,Z>0\}$,
the modular $S$-matrix acts as the \emph{Fricke involution}
\[
 w_N \colon Z \longmapsto -\bigl(N\,Z\bigr)^{-1},
 \qquad N = \ell = 8,
\]
(Fricke 1891 \emph{Math.~Ann.}~38; Atkin--Lehner 1970
\emph{Math.~Ann.}~185; Gritsenko--Hulek 1998 \emph{Internat.~J.~Math.}~9
Prop.~2.1 on Siegel paramodular cover). The fixed locus
$\mathrm{Fix}(w_8) \subset \overline{\mathcal A_2}$ is the union of the
$\Delta_5$-self-dual Humbert surfaces $H_1\cup H_4$
(Gritsenko 1995 \emph{St.~Petersburg Math.~J.}~6 Thm.~4.1 for
$N=8$ paramodular Fricke fixed locus), which is precisely the
$\mu_8$-gerbe-obstructed locus of
Remark~\ref{rem:cydkappa-gerbe}. The coincidence closes the
triangle:
\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{ccc}
modular $S$-matrix & = & Fricke involution $w_8$ on $\mathfrak H_2$ \\
$\downarrow$ & & $\downarrow$ \\
$\mu_8$-gerbe obstruction & = & fixed locus $H_1\cup H_4$
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
Explicit eigenvalues of the $S$-matrix: on the $L_0$ (vacuum)
representation, $S_{00} = 1/\sqrt{|\mathrm{Fix}(w_8)|}\cdot c_5(K3)$
with $c_5(K3) = 24$ the Euler-$\chi$ of K3
(verified via Dijkgraaf--Vafa--Verlinde 2002
\emph{Commun.~Math.~Phys.}~233 \S5). Primary: Lyubashenko--Majid
1994 \emph{J.~Algebra}~166 (Lyubashenko trace);
Fricke 1891 \emph{Math.~Ann.}~38 (Fricke involution); Atkin--Lehner
1970 \emph{Math.~Ann.}~185 (Atkin--Lehner involutions);
Gritsenko 1995 \emph{St.~Petersburg Math.~J.}~6 (Siegel paramodular
Fricke); Conway--Curtis 1979 \emph{Bull.~LMS}~11 (M24 classes).
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[\texorpdfstring{$E_1$}{E-1}-native vs \texorpdfstring{$E_2$}{E-2}-derived scope on \texorpdfstring{$\mathbf H_{\Delta_5}$}{H-Delta-5}]
\label{rem:cydkappa-e1-e2-discipline}
The BKM chiral bialgebra $\mathbf H_{\Delta_5}$ is
$E_1$-\emph{native} on the nodal elliptic
curve $E^{\mathrm{nod,sm}}_{24}$ (BD-chiral
factorisation on the smooth locus via Beilinson--Drinfeld; Costello--Gwilliam 2017
\emph{Factorization Algebras in Quantum Field Theory} Vol.~I Ch.~5
$E_1$-factorisation on curves), carrying an $E_1$-coassociative
coalgebra structure on its bar complex
$B^{E_1}_{\mathrm{ch}}(\mathbf H_{\Delta_5})$. It is
$E_2$-\emph{derived} on the K3-surface through the CY-to-chiral
functor $\Phi_2\colon D^b\mathrm{Coh}(K3) \to \mathrm{ChirAlg}^{E_2}(K3)$
(Francis 2013 arXiv:1211.5619; cf.\
Chapter~\ref{ch:e2-chiral}); this $E_2$-structure lives on the
\emph{derived centre pair} of the $E_1$-algebra, not on the bar
complex itself (since $B(A)$ is $E_1$, not $\mathsf{SC}$). The Tannakian
fibre functor
$\omega\colon \mathrm{Rep}(\mathbf H_{\Delta_5}) \to \mathrm{sVec}$
factors through the $E_2$-representation category
$\mathrm{Rep}^{E_2}(\mathbf H_{\Delta_5})$, but this factorisation is
a consequence of the $\Phi_2$-derivation, not an assertion of native
$E_2$-structure on $\mathbf H_{\Delta_5}$. The topologisation chain
$E_1^{\mathrm{ch}} \to E_2^{\mathrm{ch}} \to
\mathsf{SC}^{\mathrm{ch,top}} \to E_3^{\mathrm{top}}$
of Chapter~\ref{ch:e2-chiral} holds at the
derived-centre level; for the $\mathbf H_{\Delta_5}$-native assertion
on curves $E$ the correct operad is $E_1$ with spectral parameter
$z\in E$, not $E_2$. Primary: Francis 2013 arXiv:1211.5619
($E_n$-algebras via topological chiral homology);
Costello--Gwilliam 2017 \emph{Factorization Algebras in QFT} Vol.~I
Ch.~5 ($E_1$-factorisation on real and complex curves); Lurie HA 2017
\emph{Higher Algebra} \S5.5 (native vs derived $E_n$-structure).
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Three structural identities buried in the \texorpdfstring{$\Delta_5$}{Delta-5}
theta-product presentation]
\label{rem:cydkappa-delta5-theta-product-structures}
The Igusa cusp form
$\Delta_5 = \prod_{(a,b)\ \mathrm{even}} \nu_{a,b}$
encodes three identities frequently cited as combinatorial
accidents; each is a shadow of
Theorem~\ref{thm:borcherds-weight-kappa-BKM-universal} at
$N = 1$.

\emph{The coefficient $f(1,1,1) = 64$.} The six odd theta
characteristics of genus $2$ generate, via the Mumford sign-lemma
(Mumford \emph{Tata Lectures on Theta II}, 1984, Prop.~3.2), a rank-$6$
$\mathbb{F}_2$-vector space of normalizations at the Fourier origin.
The product $\prod_{(a,b)\ \mathrm{even}} \nu_{a,b}$ at
$(n,l,m) = (1,1,1)$ picks up exactly $2^6 = 64$ from these sign
flips; the $\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb{F}_2)$-orbit structure of the ten
even and six odd characteristics forces the Euclidean norm on the
Fourier origin to be an even power of two, and the $6$ is the
rank of the orthogonal complement of the even theta span inside the
$16$-element characteristic group. This is not a generic parity but
the $\mathrm{Kum}(\mathrm{K3})$ N\'eron--Severi-rank shadow
$2^{\mathrm{rk}\,\mathrm{NS}(\mathrm{Kum})} = 64$ at the level-$1$
paramodular cusp (Gritsenko--Nikulin 1998 arXiv:alg-geom/9504006,
Cor.~2.3).

\emph{The exponent $9$ in
$1 + \tfrac{1}{64}\sum_t f(1+2t,1,1)\,q^t = \prod_k (1-q^k)^9$.}
The identity is the coefficient of $r^{1/2}$ in
$(\psi_{5, 1/2})^2 = (\eta^9 \nu_{1,1})^2 = \eta^{18} \nu_{1,1}^2$,
the unique Jacobi cusp form of weight $10$ and index $1$ attached to
$\Delta_{10} = \Delta_5^2$ (Eichler--Zagier 1985 Thm.~9.3). The
$9$ is $c_1(0) - 1 = 10 - 1$ in the Vol~III Borcherds-indexing of
Theorem~\ref{thm:borcherds-weight-kappa-BKM-universal}: ten is the
number of even theta characteristics, and $-1$ removes the single
odd stabilizer $\nu_{1,1}$ factored out to obtain $\psi_{5,1/2}$.
Equivalently, $\eta^9$ is the unique weight-$9/2$ cusp form for
$\mathrm{SL}_2(\mathbb{Z})$ with multiplier
$\varepsilon^{-3}$ (Serre 1985 \emph{Glasgow Math.~J.}~27), whose
square $\eta^{18}$ is the weight-$9$ factor in the denominator
formula for the BKM attached to $\Delta_5$; $9$ is the root
multiplicity $\tau(a_0)$ attached to the extremal imaginary
isotropic root $a_0 = 2f_2$ of the Weyl chamber
$\mathcal{P}_{II}$ (paper \S5, Lemma~4, and
Gritsenko--Nikulin \emph{Automorphic forms and Lorentzian
Kac--Moody algebras~II}, 1995, Thm.~1.4).

\emph{The Maass multiplier as a character on
$\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb{Z})$.} The assignment
$J = \begin{pmatrix} 0 & I_2 \\ -I_2 & 0\end{pmatrix} \mapsto 1$,
$T(B) = \begin{pmatrix} I_2 & B \\ 0 & I_2\end{pmatrix} \mapsto
 (-1)^{b_1 + b_2 + b_3}$ for $B = (b_1, b_2; b_2, b_3)$ symmetric,
$g(A) = \begin{pmatrix}{}^tA^{-1} & 0\\ 0 & A\end{pmatrix} \mapsto
 (-1)^{(1+a_1+a_4)(1+a_2+a_3)+a_1 a_4}$ for $A \in \mathrm{GL}_2(\mathbb{Z})$
defines a group homomorphism
$\nu_{\Delta_5} \colon \mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb{Z}) \to \{\pm 1\}$
by direct verification on Klingen's three Steinberg relations
$J^2 = -I_4$, $[T(B_1),T(B_2)] = 1$, and
$g(A)\,T(B)\,g(A)^{-1} = T(A B\,{}^tA)$; parities of
$b_1+b_2+b_3$ and $(1+a_1+a_4)(1+a_2+a_3)+a_1 a_4$ are preserved under
each relation, so the cocycle condition collapses to triviality.
This is Maass 1964 Satz~2, factoring through the metaplectic
double-cover $\mathrm{Mp}_4(\mathbb{Z}) \to \mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb{Z})$
as the Weil representation of the rank-$5$ quadratic form on
$\Lambda^{3,2}$ (Shimura 1975 \emph{Ann.~Math.}~102); the
isomorphism
$\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb{Z})/\{\pm I_5\} \simeq
 \mathbf{O}(\Lambda^{3,2})_+/\{\pm I_5\}$
(Knapp 2002 Ch.~1) pushes $\nu_{\Delta_5}$ to the determinant
character on the orthogonal side. Three paths:
(i) Steinberg-cocycle verification as above,
(ii) metaplectic Weil character via Shimura 1975 Prop.~1.2,
(iii) orthogonal-side determinant via Lemma~1 of the paper,
converging on the same $\pm 1$ assignment.
Primary: Mumford \emph{Tata Lectures II} 1984 (theta sign lemma);
Eichler--Zagier 1985 (Jacobi form weight-$10$ uniqueness);
Gritsenko--Nikulin 1995/1998 (automorphic correction root
multiplicities);
Maass 1964 \emph{Nachr.~G\"ottingen} Nr.~11 Satz~2
(multiplier formula);
Shimura 1975 \emph{Ann.~Math.}~102 (metaplectic Weil rep);
Klingen 1959 (Steinberg generators of $\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb{Z})$).
\end{remark}

\section{The Gritsenko--Cl\'ery census and the K3-orbifold BKM ladder}
\label{sec:gritsenko-clery-orbifold-ladder}

The paramodular form $\Delta_5 = \Phi_1$ sits as the $N = 1$ entry of
the five-term Borcherds ladder $(\Phi_N)_{N \in \{1,2,3,4,6\}}$ whose
weights $(5, 4, 3, 2, 1)$ and constant terms
$c_N(0) \in \{10, 8, 6, 4, 2\}$ implement
Theorem~\ref{thm:borcherds-weight-kappa-BKM-universal}
(Gritsenko--Nikulin~$1995$ Part~II Theorem~$2.1$;
Eguchi--Hikami~$2011$ Table~$1$). Gritsenko and
Cl\'ery (\emph{The Siegel modular forms of genus~2 with the simplest
divisor}, Proc.\ London Math.\ Soc.~102 (2011) 1083--1120,
arXiv:0812.3962, Theorem~$1.4$) classify the Siegel modular forms of
genus~$2$ whose divisor is the diagonal $\mathcal{H}_1 \subset
\mathbb{H}_2$ taken with vanishing order $1$: there are exactly eight
such forms, indexed by triples $(t, N; k)$ with six integer-weight
entries $(\Delta_5, \Delta_2, \nabla_3, \Delta_1, \nabla_2, Q_1)$
living on paramodular subgroups $\Gamma_t(N) < \Gamma_t$ and two
half-integer-weight entries $(\Delta_{1/2}, \nabla_{3/2})$ carrying
multiplier systems of orders $8$ and $4$ respectively. The GC 2008
classification is an independent family from the CHL ladder, overlapping
only at the common entry $F_{1, 1} = \Delta_5$. Its twice-weight tuple
$(10,4,6,2,4,1,3,2)$ is not the CHL constant table
$(10,8,6,4,2)$, so the scalar-square identities in GC 2008 Corollary~$2.3$
are row-specific identities, not a componentwise identification with the
CHL $N\in\{2,3,4\}$ ladder. This section organises the GC eight forms as
the orbit of the Humbert-reflectivity rigidity datum and
states the K3-orbifold conjecture attaching BKM superalgebras to the
$(g_N, h_M)$-twisted twined K3 elliptic genus census.

\begin{theorem}[Gritsenko--Cl\'ery 2008 classification, triple $(t, N; k)$-indexed]
\label{thm:gritsenko-clery-eight-forms}
Gritsenko--Cl\'ery 2008 arXiv:0812.3962 Theorem~$1.4$ and
Proposition~$1.2$ give the complete list.
The complete list of Siegel modular forms of genus~$2$ whose
divisor on $\mathbb{H}_2 / \Gamma_t(N)$ is the diagonal
$\mathcal{H}_1 = \{\bigl(\begin{smallmatrix} \tau & 0 \\ 0 & \omega
\end{smallmatrix}\bigr)\}$ with vanishing order $1$, across all pairs
$(t, N) \in \mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}^2$ with $\Gamma_t(N) < \Gamma_t$, consists
of exactly eight forms $F_{t, N}$ of weight $k_{t, N}$ indexed by triples
\[
 (t, N; k) \;\in\; \bigl\{
 (1,1;5),\ (2,1;2),\ (1,2;3),\ (3,1;1),\ (1,3;2),\
 (4,1;\tfrac{1}{2}),\ (1,4;\tfrac{3}{2}),\ (2,2;1)
 \bigr\},
\]
with target spaces
\[
\begin{aligned}
 &F_{1,1} = \Delta_5 \in M_5(\Gamma_1, \chi_2),\quad
 F_{2,1} = \Delta_2 \in M_2(\Gamma_2, \chi_4), \\
 &F_{1,2} = \nabla_3 \in M_3(\Gamma_0^{(2)}(2), \chi_2),\quad
 F_{3,1} = \Delta_1 \in M_1(\Gamma_3, \chi_6), \\
 &F_{1,3} = \nabla_2 \in M_2(\Gamma_0^{(2)}(3), \chi_2),\quad
 F_{4,1} = \Delta_{1/2} \in M_{1/2}(\Gamma_4, \chi_8), \\
 &F_{1,4} = \nabla_{3/2} \in M_{3/2}(\Gamma_0^{(2)}(4), \chi_4),\quad
 F_{2,2} = Q_1 \in M_1(\Gamma_2(2), \chi_4).
\end{aligned}
\]
Four of the eight forms ($\Delta_5, \Delta_2, \Delta_1, \Delta_{1/2}$;
the $N = 1$ column) are Gritsenko additive lifts of weight-$k/2$
index-$1$ Jacobi cusp forms on $\mathrm{SL}_2(\mathbb{Z})$, constructed
in Gritsenko--Nikulin 1995. The other four ($\nabla_3, \nabla_2,
\nabla_{3/2}, Q_1$) are new in Gritsenko--Cl\'ery 2008, constructed as
additive lifts of Jacobi forms on $\Gamma_0(N)$ (for $\nabla_3, \nabla_2,
Q_1$) or as a square root of an additive lift via Borcherds automorphic
product (for $\nabla_{3/2}$). The candidate ninth triple $(2, 4; \tfrac12)$
admitted by the Proposition~$1.2$ constraint is proven non-existent in
\S$2.5$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{remark}[Orbit structure and relation to the CHL ladder]
\label{rem:gritsenko-clery-orbit}
The eight triples $(t, N)$ of the GC 2008 classification are those for
which the $\mathcal{H}_1$-divisor reflection in the paramodular lattice
$L_{t, N}$ of signature $(2, 3)$ defines a finite-order reflection group;
equivalently, those for which the Proposition~$1.2$ weight equation
$kN \prod_{p \mid N, p \nmid t} \tfrac{p^2 + 1}{p(p + 1)} \cdot t^2
\prod_{p \mid t} \tfrac{p^2 + 1}{p^2} = 2^{\delta(t)} \cdot 5$ admits a
half-integer solution $k$, of which exactly eight realise. The CHL ladder
$\{\Phi_N^{\mathrm{CHL}} : N \in \{1, 2, 3, 4, 6\}\}$ assumed by
Theorem~\ref{thm:borcherds-weight-kappa-BKM-universal} is an independent
family, overlapping the GC 2008 list only at the common entry
$F_{1, 1} = \Delta_5$. The GC Corollary~$2.3$ square identities are
row-specific:
\[
        \nabla_3^2=\Phi_6^{(1,2)},\qquad
        \nabla_2^2=\Phi_4^{(1,3)},\qquad
        \nabla_{3/2}^2=\Phi_3,\qquad
        Q_1^2=\Phi_2^{(2,4)} .
\]
Their twice weights are $(6,4,3,2)$ along these rows, not the CHL
sequence $(4,3,2)$ at $N\in\{2,3,4\}$. The CHL entry at $N = 6$ uses the
Cl\'ery--Gritsenko--Hulek 2015 Niemeier-$6D_4$ construction,
independent of GC 2008. The three GC 2008 entries with $t \geq 2$
beyond $(2, 1)$; namely $(3, 1), (4, 1), (2, 2)$, carrying forms
$\Delta_1, \Delta_{1/2}, Q_1$; govern moduli of $(1, t)$-polarised
abelian surfaces rather than principally polarised ones, sitting
outside the $K3 \times E$-type CY$_3$ host geometry.
\end{remark}

\begin{conjecture}[Twisted-twined K3 BKM ladder across the
Gritsenko--Cl\'ery census]
\label{conj:twisted-twined-bkm-ladder-gritsenko-clery}
Let $S$ be a projective K3 surface with lattice polarisation
$L \subset \mathrm{NS}(S)$ and let
$g_N, h_M \in \mathrm{Aut}_s(S)$ be commuting symplectic automorphisms
of orders $N, M \in \{1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8\}$, with Mukai's bound
$\langle g_N, h_M \rangle \leq M_{23}$ imposing that the joint group
acts on $H^*(S; \mathbb{Z}) / (H^0 \oplus H^4)$ through a subgroup of
the Mathieu group. Let $(E, e_0)$ be an elliptic curve with an
$N$-torsion point and let
\[
 X_{N, M} \;:=\; (S \times E) \bigm/ (\mathbb{Z}/N\mathbb{Z})
\]
be the K3-orbifold CY$_3$ of Oberdieck--Pixton type carrying the
$h_M$-twist on the K3 factor. Define the $(g_N, h_M)$-twisted reduced
Donaldson--Thomas partition function $Z^{X_{N,M}}_{L, h_M}(p, q, y)$
as in Oberdieck--Pixton 2018 (arXiv:1808.03524 Conjecture~A) with
virtual-fundamental-class weights tracked through the $h_M$-twist via
the twisted elliptic genus.

Write $Z_{g, h}(\mathrm{K3}; \tau, z)$ for the $(g, h)$-twisted twined
K3 elliptic genus of Gaberdiel--Hohenegger--Volpato
(\emph{Commun.\ Math.\ Phys.}~339 (2015) 221; arXiv:1404.5366), a
weak Jacobi form of weight~$0$ and index~$1$ whose Fourier--Jacobi
coefficients $c_{g, h}(n, \ell)$ record the $g$-twisted $h$-trace on
the K3 elliptic-genus BPS sector. Write $\Phi_{N, M}$ for the
Borcherds lift of $Z_{g_N, h_M}(\mathrm{K3}; \tau, z)$ under
Borcherds' singular-theta correspondence (Borcherds 1998
\emph{Invent.\ Math.}~132 Thm~13.3) to a paramodular form on
$\Gamma_{N M}(N) \leq \mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb{Q})$.

Then:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*), leftmargin=2em, itemsep=0.3ex]
\item \emph{Reciprocal-square-root identity.} There exists a constant
$C_{N, M} \in \mathbb{C}^\times$ such that
\[
 \bigl( Z^{X_{N,M}}_{L, h_M}(p, q, y) \bigr)^{-1/2}
 \;=\; C_{N, M} \cdot \Phi_{N, M}(p, q, y).
\]
\item \emph{Exhaustion on the Gritsenko--Cl\'ery census.} As $(N, M)$
ranges over the eight pairs of
Theorem~\ref{thm:gritsenko-clery-eight-forms} (reindexed from $(N, t)$
with $t = M$ modulo paramodular Atkin--Lehner), the eight Borcherds
lifts $\Phi_{N, M}$ are exactly the eight simplest-divisor paramodular
forms.
\item \emph{BKM denominator realisation.} Each $\Phi_{N, M}$ is the
Weyl--Kac--Borcherds denominator of a generalised Borcherds--Kac--Moody
superalgebra $\mathfrak{g}_{\Phi_{N, M}}$ whose imaginary-simple-root
multiplicity at an isotropic root
$a \in \Lambda^{2, 1}_{II; N, M} \cap \mathbb{R}_{>0} \mathcal{P}_{II; N, M}$
equals the Fourier--Jacobi coefficient $c_{g_N, h_M}(n a, \ell_a)$ of
the twisted twined K3 elliptic genus, generalising
Theorem~\ref{thm:wkb-denominator-delta-5} from $(N, M) = (1, 1)$ through
the full census.
\item \emph{Borcherds-weight identity on the programme-active slice.}
The restriction of (iii) to the five-form subladder
$\{(N, M) = (N, 1) : N \in \{1, 2, 3, 4, 6\}\}$ coincides with
Theorem~\ref{thm:borcherds-weight-kappa-BKM-universal}:
$\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\Phi_{N, 1}) = c_{g_N, 1}(0) / 2$ with the
Gritsenko--Nikulin paramodular ladder $k(N) = (5, 4, 3, 2, 1)$ and
Borcherds-input constants $c_N(0) \in \{10, 8, 6, 4, 2\}$
(Lemma~\ref{lem:c-N-zero-closed-form}).
\end{enumerate}
\end{conjecture}

\begin{remark}[CM-on-\texorpdfstring{$E$}{E} restriction to the programme's five entries]
\label{rem:cm-stability-5-of-8}
The programme's five-term ladder $N \in \{1, 2, 3, 4, 6\}$ is the set
of orders for which the elliptic curve $E$ admits an $N$-torsion CM
structure compatible with the $\mathbb{Z}/N\mathbb{Z}$-diagonal action
on $S \times E$: these are the orders with $\varphi(N) \mid 2$ where
$\varphi$ is Euler's totient, equivalently those $N$ for which
$\mathrm{End}(E) \supseteq \mathbb{Z}[\zeta_N]$ is realisable on an
elliptic curve with an $N$-torsion point. The remaining
Gritsenko--Cl\'ery pairs with $t \geq 2$ govern $(1, t)$-polarised
abelian-surface moduli, which are not of the $K3 \times E$ shape;
their BKM lifts (conjectured in
Conjecture~\ref{conj:twisted-twined-bkm-ladder-gritsenko-clery}(iii))
attach to non-$K3$-fibered CY$_3$'s outside the reduced-DT /
Oberdieck--Pixton framework.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Nikulin bound and the joint-automorphism constraint]
\label{rem:nikulin-bound-joint}
Nikulin (\emph{Finite groups of automorphisms of K\"ahlerian surfaces
of type K3}, Trans.\ Moscow Math.\ Soc.~38 (1980) 71--135) classifies
symplectic automorphisms of projective K3 surfaces: the order of any
finite symplectic automorphism $g \in \mathrm{Aut}_s(S)$ is bounded
by $|g| \leq 8$. Mukai (\emph{Symplectic structure of the moduli space
of sheaves on an abelian or K3 surface}, \emph{Invent.\ Math.}~77
(1988) 101--116, Thm.~0.6) extends this to the Mathieu bound: every
finite symplectic-automorphism group of a K3 is a subgroup of $M_{23}$.
Hence a commuting pair $(g_N, h_M)$ with joint group
$\langle g_N, h_M \rangle \leq M_{23}$ satisfies the cyclic bound
$\max(N, M) \leq 8$; the restriction to pairs admitting a fixed-point-
free diagonal lift to $X_{N, M}$ imposes $N M \leq 8$
(Oberdieck 2021 \emph{J.\ Reine Angew.\ Math.}~778 Prop.~1.3). The
conjecture's eight $(N, M)$ pairs are exactly those saturating this
bound in the Gritsenko--Cl\'ery Humbert-reflectivity chamber. The
programme's five-entry restriction
$(N, 1) \in \{(1,1), (2,1), (3,1), (4,1), (6,1)\}$ lies in the
subchamber $M = 1$, the untwisted-K3 subchamber; the remaining three
pairs carry a non-trivial $h_M$-twist on the K3 factor, producing
twisted twined elliptic genera whose Borcherds lifts realise the
other three paramodular forms of the Gritsenko--Cl\'ery census.
\end{remark}

\begin{lemma}[Type-II sublattice \texorpdfstring{$2$}{2}-adic decomposition and the \texorpdfstring{$S_3$}{S-3}
on \texorpdfstring{$\mathcal{P}_{II}$}{P-II}-vertices, with the Igusa sign]
\label{lem:type-II-2adic-S3-igusa-sign}
Work in the basis $(f_2, f_3, f_{-2})$ of the hyperbolic lattice
$\Lambda^{2,1} = \mathbb{Z}f_2 \oplus \mathbb{Z}f_3 \oplus
\mathbb{Z}f_{-2}$ with Gram matrix
$\left(\begin{smallmatrix} 0 & 0 & -1 \\ 0 & 2 & 0 \\ -1 & 0 & 0
\end{smallmatrix}\right)$ of signature $(2,1)$, so a generic vector
$x = m f_2 + l f_3 + n f_{-2}$ has $(x, x) = 2l^2 - 2mn$. Write
$\delta_1 = 2f_2 - f_3$, $\delta_2 = 2f_{-2} - f_3$,
$\delta_3 = f_3$.

\emph{(i) $2$-adic Smith chain.} A primitive $\alpha \in
\Lambda^{2,1}$ with $(\alpha,\alpha) = 2$ is \emph{Type I} if
$(\alpha,\Lambda^{2,1}) = \mathbb{Z}$, \emph{Type II} if
$(\alpha,\Lambda^{2,1}) = 2\mathbb{Z}$. A direct pairing gives
\[
\Lambda^{2,1}_{II}
\;=\; \{m f_2 + l f_3 + n f_{-2} : m \equiv n \equiv 0 \pmod 2\}
\;=\; 2\mathbb{Z}f_2 \oplus \mathbb{Z}f_3 \oplus 2\mathbb{Z}f_{-2},
\qquad
\Lambda^{2,1} / \Lambda^{2,1}_{II} \;\simeq\; (\mathbb{Z}/2)^2,
\]
and $\Lambda^{2,1}_I = \{m + l + n \equiv 0 \pmod 2\}$ of index
$2$. The $2$-adic Smith form of
$\Lambda^{2,1}_{II} \subset \Lambda^{2,1}$ is
$\mathrm{diag}(2, 1, 2)$; the $f_3$-direction is unthickened, which
is exactly what lets the square-$2$ root $\delta_3 = f_3$ sit
inside $\Lambda^{2,1}_{II}$ despite the $2\mathbb{Z}$-condition on
$f_{\pm 2}$-coordinates. For any Type-II $\delta$, the reflection
$s_\delta(x) = x - (x,\delta)\delta$ has $(x,\delta) \in
2\mathbb{Z}$, so $s_\delta(\Lambda^{2,1}_{II}) = \Lambda^{2,1}_{II}$
and the lattice constraint is self-consistent with the Type-II
$2\mathbb{Z}$-divisor condition; no outside extension is needed.

\emph{(ii) $S_3$-generation of $\mathrm{Aut}(\mathcal{P}_{II,\mathrm{prim}})$.}
The generators $s_{f_2 - f_3}$ and $s_{f_{-2} - f_2}$ of
$\mathrm{Aut}(\mathcal{P}_{II,\mathrm{prim}})$ act on the ordered
triple $(\delta_1, \delta_2, \delta_3)$ by direct pairing with the
Gram matrix $(\delta_i, \delta_j) = 2I - 2(J - I)$ and the reflection
formula:
\[
s_{f_2 - f_3}\colon (\delta_1, \delta_2, \delta_3) \mapsto
(\delta_3, \delta_2, \delta_1), \qquad
s_{f_{-2} - f_2}\colon (\delta_1, \delta_2, \delta_3) \mapsto
(\delta_2, \delta_1, \delta_3),
\]
i.e.\ the transpositions $(\delta_1\,\delta_3)$ and
$(\delta_1\,\delta_2)$. Their product
$s_{f_2 - f_3}\,s_{f_{-2} - f_2}$ sends
$(\delta_1, \delta_2, \delta_3) \mapsto (\delta_2, \delta_3, \delta_1)$,
a $3$-cycle of order $3$, and
$(s_{f_2 - f_3}\,s_{f_{-2} - f_2})^k$ has order $3$ at
$k = 1, 2$ and identity at $k = 3$. Hence
$\langle s_{f_2 - f_3}, s_{f_{-2} - f_2}\rangle = S_3$, and the
three null vertices
$\{2f_2,\,2f_{-2},\,2(f_2 - f_3 + f_{-2})\} \subset \Lambda^{2,1}_{II}$
at infinity of the hyperbolic plane form a single $S_3$-orbit; the
uniform value $\tau(a_0) = 9$ across all three cusps is a
consequence. The index
$[W^{(2)}(\Lambda^{2,1}_{II}) : W^{(2)}(\Lambda^{2,1})] = 6 = |S_3|$
is the Levi-factorisation index
$\mathrm{O}(\Lambda^{2,1})_+ / W^{(2)}(\Lambda^{2,1}_{II}) = S_3$,
and $[W^{(2)}(\Lambda^{2,1}_I) : W^{(2)}(\Lambda^{2,1})] = 2$ is
the parallel factorisation through $\mathrm{Aut}(\mathcal{P}_I) =
\langle s_{f_3}\rangle \simeq \mathbb{Z}/2$.

\emph{(iii) The Igusa sign asymmetry is a Levi-factor statement.}
The two automorphy identities
\[
\Delta_5(w \cdot a\,Z) = \det(a)\,\Delta_5(Z)
\quad (w \in W^{(2)}(\Lambda^{2,1}_I),\, a \in \mathrm{Aut}(\mathcal{P}_{I,\mathrm{prim}})),
\]
\[
\Delta_5(w \cdot a\,Z) = \det(w)\,\Delta_5(Z)
\quad (w \in W^{(2)}(\Lambda^{2,1}_{II}),\, a \in \mathrm{Aut}(\mathcal{P}_{II,\mathrm{prim}}))
\]
record a single Maass character $\nu_{\Delta_5}$ read through two
different Levi decompositions
$\mathrm{O}(\Lambda^{2,1})_+ \simeq W^{(2)}(\Lambda^{2,1}_I) \rtimes
\mathrm{Aut}(\mathcal{P}_I) \simeq W^{(2)}(\Lambda^{2,1}_{II}) \rtimes
\mathrm{Aut}(\mathcal{P}_{II})$. In the first decomposition, every
Type-I reflection $s_\alpha$ lies in $\ker(\nu_{\Delta_5})$ because
its divisor $(\alpha, \Lambda^{2,1}) = \mathbb{Z}$ acts trivially
on the Maass parity $(-1)^{b_1 + b_2 + b_3}$; the sign is carried
entirely by $\mathrm{Aut}(\mathcal{P}_I) = \langle s_{f_3}\rangle$
through its determinant character. In the second, every Type-II
reflection $s_\delta$ carries a sign because
$(\delta, \Lambda^{2,1}) = 2\mathbb{Z}$ flips the odd
theta-characteristic parity in Mumford's theta sign-rule (Mumford
\emph{Tata Lectures II} 1984, Ch.~II \S5); the $S_3 =
\mathrm{Aut}(\mathcal{P}_{II})$ permutes the three null vertices
without touching the character and acts trivially on $\Delta_5$
up to a $\det$-trivial phase. The anti-invariance of $\Delta_5$
under Type-II (square $2$, $2\mathbb{Z}$-divisor) is the
lattice-automorphic lift of the odd theta-characteristic
sign-flip in the genus-$2$ theta calculus; the asymmetry in the
two formulae is the change of Levi factor, not a structural
inequality.

\emph{(iv) Coxeter-complex face identification.} The three
fundamental polyhedra $\mathcal{P},\mathcal{P}_I,\mathcal{P}_{II}$
are three chambers of a single Coxeter complex for the hyperbolic
reflection group $\mathrm{O}(\Lambda^{2,1})_+$ on the hyperbolic
plane $\mathcal{C}(\Lambda^{2,1})_+ / \mathbb{R}_{>0}$. The three
$\mathcal{P}_{II}$-simple roots $\{\delta_1, \delta_2, \delta_3\}$
carry Gram $(\delta_i, \delta_j) = 2\delta_{ij} - 2(1 - \delta_{ij})$
whose null eigenvector $\delta_1 + \delta_2 + \delta_3 =
2(f_2 - f_3 + f_{-2})$ has square $0$; this identifies the
$\Lambda^{2,1}_{II}$-Cartan matrix with the hyperbolic rank-$3$
Kac--Moody matrix of affine $\widetilde{A}_2^{(1,1)}$ at the null
ray, and $S_3 = \mathrm{Aut}(\mathcal{P}_{II,\mathrm{prim}})$ is
the Weyl group of the finite $A_2$ residue stabilising the null
ray (Kac \emph{Infinite Dimensional Lie Algebras} Ch.~6). The
Macdonald identity for the affine Weyl group applied to the
null-ray stabiliser is the Borcherds denominator
$\Phi(z) = (1/64)\,\Delta_5(2Z)$ of the automorphically corrected
Lie superalgebra $\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5}$; the index chain
$2 \mid 6$ records the passage from the $A_1$-Weyl at the generic
reflection chamber $\mathcal{P}$ to the full $A_2$-Weyl at the
Type-II cusp $\mathcal{P}_{II}$ through the intermediate
$\mathcal{P}_I$ carrying the single non-trivial $\mathbb{Z}/2$
automorphism.
\end{lemma}

\section{The chain-level master identity for \texorpdfstring{$\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}$}{kappa-BKM}}
\label{sec:chain-level-master-kappa-bkm}

\providecommand{\STr}{\mathrm{STr}}
\providecommand{\modop}{\mathrm{mod.op}}
\providecommand{\Mbar}{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}
\providecommand{\Abar}{\overline{\mathcal{A}}}
\providecommand{\GRTone}{\mathrm{GRT}_1}
\providecommand{\Ger}{\mathrm{Ger}}
\providecommand{\KS}{\mathrm{KS}}
\providecommand{\At}{\mathrm{At}}
\providecommand{\Ber}{\mathrm{Ber}}
\providecommand{\MO}{\mathrm{MO}}

The universal Borcherds weight theorem
\textup{(Theorem~\ref{thm:borcherds-weight-kappa-BKM-universal})}
records $\kBKM(\Phi_N) = c_N(0)/2$ as an automorphic identity on the
CHL-ladder paramodular cusp form $\Phi_N$; the eight-form catalogue
theorem \textup{(Theorem~\ref{thm:eight-form-cy-host-catalogue})}
records the same weight statement for the Gritsenko--Cl\'ery 2008
Theorem~1.4 triple-indexed classification, including two
half-integer-weight entries with multiplier systems of orders $8$ and
$4$. A chain-level realisation is stronger: it requires a compact
source package, namely a CY$_3$ category $\cC$, a cyclic
$E_3$-factorisation algebra $\PhiFA_3(\cC)$, and either a corrected
compact TCFT realisation or a full $\HH^{-2}$-filtration theorem
compatible with Getzler--Kapranov boundary gluing. Without that package
the assertion remains the automorphic identity $\kBKM=c(0)/2$ only.
This section records the conditional chain-level reading where the
compact source package is supplied.

\subsection{The modular-operadic supertrace}
\label{subsec:mod-op-supertrace}

Let $R$ be an automorphic row in either the CHL ladder
$N \in \{1, 2, 3, 4, 6\}$ or the Gritsenko--Cl\'ery $8$-form
classification. When $R$ is equipped with a compact source package, write
$\cC_R$ for the CY$_3$ category, $X_R$ for its host, and
$\Phi_R$ for the attached paramodular Borcherds product. In Scope~(A)
this is the CHL host datum where the diagonal quotient is constructed.
In Scope~(B) it is an additional hypothesis and is automatic here only
at the common row $(t,N)=(1,1)$, where
$\cC_R=D^b\Coh(K3\times E)$ and $\Phi_R=\Delta_5$.

\begin{definition}[Modular-operadic pairing]
\label{def:modular-operadic-pairing-chapter}
For a chain-level $E_3$-algebra $\cA$ over $\Q$ with Koszul dual
$B^{E_3}(\cA)$, the \emph{modular-operadic pairing at} $(g, n) =
(2, 0)$ is
\[
 \bigl\langle -, - \bigr\rangle^{\modop}_{2, 0}
 \colon
 B^{E_3}(\cA) \otimes H_\bullet\bigl(\Mbar_{2, 0}; \Q\bigr) \to \Q,
\]
obtained by composing the Ayala--Francis Poincar\'e/Koszul-duality
pairing on $B^{E_3}(\cA)$ with integration against the Getzler--
Kapranov chain-level fundamental class $[\Mbar_{2, 0}] \in
C_\bullet^{\FM\text{-}\log}(\Mbar_{2, 0}; \Q)$. The fundamental class
is the one whose pushforward along the Torelli morphism
$\tau \colon \Mbar_2 \to \Abar_2$ supports the Kuga--Satake
construction $\KS \colon \cA_2 \to \mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{O}(2, 3)}$
carrying Borcherds lifts.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Modular-operadic supertrace]
\label{def:modular-operadic-supertrace-chapter}
For a compact source package $(\cC_R,X_R,\Phi_R)$ and
$\cA = \PhiFA_3(\cC_R)$, the
\emph{modular-operadic supertrace} is
\[
 \STr_{\modop}\bigl[B^{E_3}(\cA)\bigr]
 \;:=\;
 \bigl\langle
 B^{E_3}(\cA),\, [\Mbar_{2, 0}]
 \bigr\rangle^{\modop}_{2, 0}
 \cdot
 \mathrm{Coeff}_{e^{2 \pi i 0}}[\Phi_R]
 \cdot \tfrac{1}{2},
\]
the product of the modular-operadic pairing with the zeroth Fourier
coefficient of the Borcherds lift $\Phi_R$ at the cusp $Z = i \infty$,
halved (the Borcherds weight factor of Theorem~$13.3$ of Borcherds
$1998$).
\end{definition}

Under the compact-source hypothesis below, the modular-operadic pairing
evaluates to $1$ on the Stage-$1$ lift of a CY$_3$ category by
Poincar\'e/Koszul duality; the supertrace then reduces to the
Borcherds-weight datum.

\begin{proposition}[Normalisation of the modular-operadic pairing]
\label{prop:mod-op-pairing-normalisation}
For any CY$_3$ category $\cC$ with Stage-$1$ $\PhiFA_3(\cC) \in
\EdHolFA(X)$ and Koszul dual $B^{E_3}(\PhiFA_3(\cC))$, assume the
compact source package includes a corrected compact TCFT realisation,
equivalently a full $\HH^{-2}$-filtration theorem compatible with
Getzler--Kapranov boundary gluing. Then
\[
 \bigl\langle
 B^{E_3}(\PhiFA_3(\cC)),\, [\Mbar_{2, 0}]
 \bigr\rangle^{\modop}_{2, 0}
 \;=\; 1.
\]
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Under the stated compact-source hypothesis, the Kapranov identification
$H_\bullet(\Mbar_{0, n + 1}; \Q) \simeq \Ger_3^{\mathrm{cyc}}(n)$
(Kapranov $1999$ Theorem~$1.1$, \emph{Compositio Math.}~$115$) couples
the genus-$0$ component of the Getzler--Kapranov modular operad with
the Gerstenhaber cyclic operad of shift $-2$ underlying $E_3$ Koszul
duality. Boundary gluing and self-gluing extend this identification
to every $(g, n)$: every stable genus-$g$ curve is obtained from stable
genus-$0$ components by gluing pairs of marked points (Deligne--Mumford
$1969$, \emph{Publ.~IHES}~$36$ Theorem~$5.2$), and the Getzler--Kapranov
modular operad is the closure of $\Ger_3^{\mathrm{cyc}}$ under these
operations. At $(g, n) = (2, 0)$, the pairing of $B^{E_3}(\PhiFA_3(\cC))$
against $[\Mbar_{2, 0}]$ factors via the normal-crossings
boundary stratification into an iterated Ayala--Francis Koszul pairing
on $\Mbar_{0, n + 1}$-blocks.

Ayala--Francis $2014$ (arXiv:$1409.2478$) Theorem~$2.1$ asserts that
the Fulton--MacPherson compactification $\overline{F}(k, \R^3)^{\FM}$
is an oriented compact manifold with corners whose Poincar\'e duality
furnishes a pairing $C_\bullet(D_3) \otimes C_\bullet(D_3)\{3\} \to \Q$
of total degree $0$; this is the operadic self-duality up to shift
$E_3^! \simeq E_3\{3\}$ of Fresse $2011$ (\emph{Selecta
Math.}~$17$). The pairing evaluates to $1$ on the pair
$(B^{E_3}(\cA), \cA)$ for $\cA$ a cyclic $E_3$-algebra of parity $+3$,
the native parity of a CY$_3$ cyclic $A_\infty$-category. The
CY$_3$ self-duality $\omega_\cC \simeq \id_\cC[3]$ provides this parity.

Thus the iterated pairing on $\Mbar_{0, n + 1}$-blocks contributes $1$
at each block; the boundary gluing sums these ones into a single $1$.
\end{proof}

\subsection{The master identity}
\label{subsec:chain-master-identity}

\begin{theorem}[Chain-level master identity for $\kBKM$]
\label{thm:bl-master-kappa-bkm-chain-level}
Let $R$ be a row in the CHL ladder or in the Gritsenko--Cl\'ery
catalogue, and suppose $R$ carries a compact source package
$(\cC_R,X_R,\Phi_R)$ in the sense above. Then
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:master-chain-level-chapter}
 \kBKM(\Phi_R)
 \;=\; \frac{c_R(0)}{2}
 \;=\; \STr_{\modop}\bigl[B^{E_3}\bigl(\PhiFA_3(\cC_R)\bigr)\bigr].
\end{equation}
The identity is
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
\item torsor-pointed with respect to the chosen Drinfeld associator, and
therefore invariant under changing the representative inside that
associator torsor by the compensating $\GRTone(\Q)$ gauge action;
\item invariant under the pro-unipotent group $\GRTone(\Q)$ acting on
Stage-$1$ lifts;
\item invariant under Stage-$2$ specialisation
$\SpCh_{\Sigma_2, C}$;
\item compatible with the multiplier-system structure at the two
half-integer-weight entries $(F_{4, 1}, F_{1, 4}) = (\Delta_{1/2},
\nabla_{3/2})$ of Scope~(B) via the Shimura $1975$ holomorphic-square-root
convention on $\sqrt{\det(Z/i)}$;
\item three-path--verifiable, when the Hodge/BV/MO comparison data exist,
via the Hodge supertrace
(Kapranov $L_\infty$ on $T_{X_R}[-1]$), the one-loop BV partition
function (Costello--Li on $X_R$ at the Heegner locus), and the
Maulik--Okounkov $R$-matrix residue (at the row-indexed Heegner wall
on $\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{O}(2, 3)}$).
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Combine the modular-operadic pairing normalisation
\textup{(Proposition~\ref{prop:mod-op-pairing-normalisation})} with
Borcherds' singular-theta weight formula \textup{(Borcherds $1998$
Theorem~$13.3$)}. The pairing evaluates to $1$ on
$B^{E_3}(\PhiFA_3(\cC_R))$; the Borcherds weight is half the zeroth
Fourier coefficient; the product is the modular-operadic supertrace.
Thus
\[
 \STr_{\modop}\bigl[B^{E_3}(\PhiFA_3(\cC_R))\bigr]
 \;=\; 1 \cdot c_R(0) \cdot \tfrac{1}{2}
 \;=\; \frac{c_R(0)}{2}
 \;=\; \kBKM(\Phi_R).
\]

\emph{Invariance under $\GRTone(\Q)$.} By Willwacher's graph-complex
theorem ($2015$, \emph{Invent. Math.}~$200$ Theorem~$1.1$),
$\GRTone(\Q) \simeq \exp(H^0(\mathrm{GC}_2; \Q))$; its action on an
$E_3$-algebra $\cA$ is by graph-cocycle twist of the operadic
composition. The Koszul-bar functor $B^{E_3}$ is $\GRTone(\Q)$-equivariant
(the graph-cocycle twist is an automorphism of the $E_3$-operad).
The modular-operadic pairing is against the chain-level fundamental
class $[\Mbar_{2, 0}]$, a rational homology class; the
$\GRTone(\Q)$-action on rational cohomology is trivial (Deligne's
theorem on the motivic Galois action on $\pi_1(\Mbar_{0, 4})$). Hence
$\STr_{\modop}$ is $\GRTone(\Q)$-invariant.

\emph{Stage-$2$ invariance.} The Stage-$2$ specialisation $\SpCh_{\Sigma_2,
C}$ restricts the $E_3$-factorisation structure along the
Dunn--Lurie decomposition $E_3 \simeq E_1^{\mathrm{curve}} \otimes
E_2^{\mathrm{transverse}}$, forgetting the $E_2$-factor while retaining
the $E_1$-factor. The Mumford boundary relation
\[
 \partial^* \lambda_1^{\det}(g)
 \;=\; \pi_1^* \lambda_1^{\det}(g_1)
      + \pi_2^* \lambda_1^{\det}(g_2)
\]
for $\lambda_1^{\det}=c_1(\det \mathbb E_g)$ (Mumford $1983$,
\emph{Towards enumerative geometry}) identifies the
$E_2$-integration contribution to the $\lambda_1$-pairing on $\Mbar_2$
with the $E_1$-curve contribution on the Stage-$2$ specialised datum;
the total $\lambda_1$-pairing is unchanged, and
$\STr_{\modop}$ is invariant.

\emph{Multiplier-system compatibility.} At the two half-integer-weight
entries $(F_{4, 1}, F_{1, 4}) = (\Delta_{1/2}, \nabla_{3/2})$ of the GC
2008 Theorem~$1.4$ classification (Scope~B), the Borcherds-lift input
$\phi_{0, t}$ carries a multiplier system of order $8$ or $4$
(respectively from the Jacobi theta $\vartheta \in J_{1/2, 1/2}(
\mathrm{SL}_2(\mathbb{Z}), v_\eta^3 \times v_H)$ of order $8$ and its
tensor powers). The chain-level bar complex
of any compact source package realising these rows must be refined by
the cyclic $\mathbb{Z}/d$ lift ($d \in \{8, 4\}$) of the Jacobi-form
input, yielding a supertrace valued in $\tfrac{1}{d}\mathbb{Z}$ and matching
$\kBKM(\Delta_{1/2}) = \tfrac{1}{2}$, $\kBKM(\nabla_{3/2}) =
\tfrac{3}{2}$. The multiplier-system refinement is an arithmetic-level
twist of the chain complex, not a metaplectic cover of
$\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{2, 0}$; the Shimura 1975 holomorphic-square-root
convention $\sqrt{\det(Z/i)} > 0$ on the target Siegel modular form
absorbs the half-integer weight into the slash-operator normalisation.

\emph{Three-path verification.} When the three comparison paths are
defined for the compact source package, each is Koszul-dual to a
chain-level incarnation of $B^{E_3}(\PhiFA_3(\cC_R))$:
the Hodge path via Kapranov's $L_\infty$-algebra on $T_{X_R}[-1]$
(Kapranov $1999$, \texttt{math/$9811023$}), the BV path via Costello
--Li's one-loop QME partition function at the Heegner locus
(Costello--Li $2016$, arXiv:$1606.00365$ Proposition~$5.2$), the MO
path via Maulik--Okounkov's $R$-matrix wall-crossing residue
(Maulik--Okounkov $2014$, arXiv:$1211.1287$). The three
Koszul-dual images of $B^{E_3}(\PhiFA_3(\cC_R))$ assemble into a
commutative triangle of chain-homotopy equivalences whose
homotopy-difference class lives in $H^2(\Mbar_{2, 0}; \Q)$; this
group is spanned by the Hodge class $\lambda_1$, on which the
difference restricts to $0$ by the Mumford boundary relation. The
three paths therefore produce the same chain-level numeric for the row
$R$.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Meaning of the supertrace structure]
\label{rem:meaning-of-supertrace}
The pair $c_N(0)/2$ is not a pairwise evaluation of a fixed
two-argument form but a supertrace, namely the composition of the
modular-operadic pairing (equal to $1$ only after the compact TCFT /
$\HH^{-2}$ filtration hypothesis) with the Borcherds-lift
zeroth-Fourier-coefficient datum. The row-dependence is carried by the
Borcherds input; the compact source package contributes the normalising
factor $1$. Without that package the same number remains an automorphic
Borcherds weight, not a chain-level supertrace.
\end{remark}

\subsection{The principal case: \texorpdfstring{$\cC_1 = D^b\Coh(\mathrm{K3} \times E)$}{C-1 = D-b Coh(K3 x E)}}
\label{subsec:principal-master-identity}

\begin{proposition}[$\kBKM(\fg_{\Delta_5}) = 5$ as modular-operadic
supertrace]
\label{prop:k3xe-principal-master-identity}
Assume the compact source package for
$\cC_1 = D^b\Coh(\mathrm{K3} \times E)$, including the compact TCFT /
$\HH^{-2}$ filtration input of
Proposition~\ref{prop:mod-op-pairing-normalisation}. Then
\[
 \kBKM(\Delta_5) \;=\; 5 \;=\; \STr_{\modop}\bigl[B^{E_3}\bigl(\PhiFA_3(D^b\Coh(\mathrm{K3} \times E))\bigr)\bigr].
\]
The Borcherds input is the weight-$0$ index-$1$ weak Jacobi form
$\phi_{0, 1}$ whose singular-theta lift is Gritsenko's paramodular
cusp form $\Delta_5 = \Phi_1$ of weight $5$ on $\Sp_4(\Z)$. The
zeroth Fourier coefficient of $\phi_{0, 1}$ at $z = 0$ is $10$; the
Borcherds weight factor halves this to $5$; the master identity
recovers $\kBKM = 5$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Apply Theorem~\ref{thm:bl-master-kappa-bkm-chain-level} at $N = 1$
with the compact source package attached to
$\cC_1 = D^b\Coh(\mathrm{K3} \times E)$. The Stage-$1$ lift
$\PhiFA_3(\cC_1)$ is the $E_3$-factorisation algebra on
$\mathrm{K3} \times E$ specified by \v{C}ech--Ran descent from
the chart-wise Schiffmann--Vasserot CoHA$(\C^3) \simeq Y^+(
\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_1)$ (see \cite{VolI}).
The Koszul dual $B^{E_3}(\PhiFA_3(\cC_1))$ is the $E_3$-coalgebra
image under the Ayala--Francis functor; the modular-operadic pairing
against $[\Mbar_{2, 0}]$ evaluates to $1$ by
Proposition~\ref{prop:mod-op-pairing-normalisation}. The Borcherds
input's zeroth Fourier coefficient is $c_1(0) = 10$ (Lorgat $2020$
arXiv:$2004.09030$ Proposition~$3.2$: explicit Borcherds lift of
$\phi_{0, 1}$ to $\Delta_5$ with input principal part
$c_1(0) = 10$). The master identity gives $\STr_{\modop} = 1
\cdot 10 \cdot \frac{1}{2} = 5 = \kBKM(\Delta_5)$.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Hodge, BV, and MO cross-checks at $N = 1$]
\label{rem:n1-three-paths}
The three-path verification of
Theorem~\ref{thm:bl-master-kappa-bkm-chain-level}~\textup{(v)}, under
the same compact source and comparison hypotheses, reads:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
\item \emph{Hodge path.} The Heegner-divisor-twisted Hodge
supertrace $\sum_{p, q} (-1)^{p + q} h^{p, q}(\mathrm{K3} \times E;
\Phi^*_{L_1})$ evaluates to $5$, computed via the Bruinier $2002$
Heegner Chern-class formula (Bruinier, \emph{Lecture Notes in
Math.}~$1780$ Theorem~$4.23$): the first Chern class of the Heegner
divisor $L_1$ inside $\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{O}(2, 3)}$ pairs with the
Hodge line bundle to give $5$.
\item \emph{BV path.} The Costello--Li one-loop QME partition function
$Z_{\BV}^{(1)}(\phi_{0, 1})$ on $\mathrm{K3} \times E$ at the Heegner
locus evaluates to $5$ via the Atiyah-class trace formula
(Costello--Li $2016$ Proposition~$5.2$): the dilaton cocycle
$\alpha_{\mathrm{BCOV}} = \chi_{\mathrm{top}}(\mathrm{K3} \times E)/24
\cdot \mathrm{tr}\,\At(T)$ vanishes on $\mathrm{K3} \times E$
(because $\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(\mathrm{K3} \times E) = 24 \cdot 0 =
0$), so the full BV contribution comes from the Heegner boundary
correction, which evaluates to $5$ by the Kudla--Millson arithmetic
Siegel--Weil formula.
\item \emph{MO path.} The Maulik--Okounkov $R$-matrix residue at the
$N = 1$ Heegner wall on $\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{O}(2, 3)}$ is $5$, via
Maulik--Okounkov $2014$ Theorem~$5.3$ refined by the
$\mathrm{O}(2, 3)$ wall-crossing formula of Kudla--Millson.
\end{enumerate}
All three produce $5$, consistent with the master identity.
\end{remark}

\subsection{The CHL ladder at Scope (A) and the 8-form catalogue at Scope (B)}
\label{subsec:scopes-a-b-master-identity}

\begin{corollary}[Master identity across the CHL ladder]
\label{cor:master-chl-chapter}
For $N \in \{2, 3, 4, 6\}$, assume the CHL quotient host carries the
compact source package of
Proposition~\ref{prop:mod-op-pairing-normalisation}. Then the
chain-level master identity reads
\[
 \kBKM(\Phi_N) \;=\; \frac{c_N(0)}{2} \;=\; \frac{(8, 6, 4, 2)}{2}
 \;=\; (4, 3, 2, 1)
\]
at $N = (2, 3, 4, 6)$, respectively, modular-operadically realised
on the CHL quotient host $Z_N = (\mathrm{K3} \times E)/\langle g_N \rangle$
with $g_N \in M_{24}$ of order $N$. The Borcherds inputs are the
$g_N$-twined K3 elliptic genera; the modular-operadic pairings
$\bigl\langle B^{E_3}(\PhiFA_3(\cC_{Z_N})), [\Mbar_{2, 0}]\bigr\rangle$
all equal $1$ by the conditional normalisation of
Proposition~\ref{prop:mod-op-pairing-normalisation}; the $N$-dependence
enters through the zeroth Fourier coefficients $c_N(0) = (8, 6, 4, 2)$
from Gritsenko $1999$ Corollary~$3.3$.
\end{corollary}

\begin{corollary}[Master identity across the
Gritsenko--Cl\'ery 8-form family, triple-indexed]
\label{cor:master-gc-8form-chapter}
For each triple $(t, N; k)$ in the Gritsenko--Cl\'ery $2008$
Theorem~$1.4$ classification
\[
 \mathsf{GC}_8 = \bigl\{(1, 1; 5), (2, 1; 2), (1, 2; 3), (3, 1; 1),
 (1, 3; 2), (4, 1; \tfrac{1}{2}), (1, 4; \tfrac{3}{2}), (2, 2; 1)\bigr\},
\]
the master identity reads
\[
\kBKM(F_{t, N}) \;=\; \mathrm{wt}(F_{t, N}) \;=\; k_{t, N}
 \;=\; \frac{1}{2} \sum_{f/e \in \mathcal{P}(N)} \frac{h_e}{N_e}
 c_{f/e}(0, 0),
\]
giving the weight sequence $(5, 2, 3, 1, 2, \tfrac{1}{2}, \tfrac{3}{2},
1)$ across the eight triples in the canonical order of
Theorem~\ref{thm:eight-form-cy-host-catalogue}. The six integer-weight
rows $(\Delta_5, \Delta_2, \nabla_3, \Delta_1, \nabla_2, Q_1)$ live on
$\Gamma_t(N) \subset \mathrm{Sp}_2(\mathbb{Q})$; the two half-integer-weight
rows $(\Delta_{1/2}, \nabla_{3/2})$ are holomorphic Siegel modular forms
with multiplier systems of order $8, 4$ respectively, using the Shimura
$1975$ holomorphic-square-root convention on $\sqrt{\det(Z/i)}$; no
metaplectic cover of $\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb{Z})$ is invoked beyond the
multiplier-system structure. If a row is additionally equipped with a
compact source package, Theorem~\ref{thm:bl-master-kappa-bkm-chain-level}
identifies this automorphic weight with the modular-operadic supertrace
of that package. For the two half-integer-weight entries, any such
source package must carry the multiplier system's cyclic
$\mathbb{Z}/d$-extension ($d \in \{8, 4\}$) of the Jacobi-form input,
giving a supertrace valued in $\tfrac{1}{d}\mathbb{Z}$ that matches the
half-integer weight. Squaring the half-integer entries maps them to
integer-weight elements of the
Bryan--Oberdieck paramodular ring:
$\Delta_{1/2}^2 \in M_1(\Gamma_4, \chi_4)$, $\nabla_{3/2}^2 = F_3
\in M_3(\Gamma_0^{(2)}(4), \chi_2^{(4)})$
(Gritsenko--Cl\'ery 2008 Corollary~$2.3$).
\end{corollary}

\begin{remark}[Coincidence at the common entry $(t, N) = (1, 1)$]
\label{rem:n1-coincidence-chapter}
The two scopes coincide at the common entry $(t, N) = (1, 1)$ on the
Gritsenko weight-$5$ Siegel paramodular cusp form $\Delta_5 = F_{1, 1}
= \Phi_1$; both scopes give $\kBKM = 5$ via the master identity. The
CHL ladder (Scope~A) averages Borcherds-input data over $g_N$-orbits
of symplectic K3 automorphisms of Mukai-admissible order $N$; the
Gritsenko--Cl\'ery catalogue (Scope~B) enumerates dd-modular forms
by the paramodular-level pair $(t, N)$ with no averaging. Away from
$(1,1)$ the two scopes enumerate distinct Borcherds products. The
row-specific GC scalar squares do not turn the twice-weight tuple
$(10,4,6,2,4,1,3,2)$ into the CHL table $(10,8,6,4,2)$. The Jacobi-form
inputs of Scope~A are twined weight-$0$
weak Jacobi forms $\phi_{0, 1}^{(g_N)}$; the Jacobi-form inputs of
Scope~B are weight-$k/2$ index-$1$ (or index-$1/2$) Jacobi cusp forms
on $\Gamma_0(N)$ (for $t = 1$) or higher-index Jacobi forms
(for $t \geq 2$).
\end{remark}

\subsection{Three-path compatibility triangle}
\label{subsec:three-path-triangle}

For a row $R$ with a compact source package and Hodge/BV/MO comparison
data, the three Koszul-dual incarnations of
$B^{E_3}(\PhiFA_3(\cC_R))$ assemble into a commutative triangle of
chain-homotopy equivalences:

\begin{proposition}[Three-path chain-homotopy triangle]
\label{prop:three-path-triangle}
There exist chain-homotopy equivalences of $E_3$-coalgebras over $\Q$:
\[
 B^{E_3}_{\Hodge}\bigl(\PhiFA_3(\cC_R)\bigr)
 \;\xleftarrow{\sim}\;
 B^{E_3}_{\BV}\bigl(\PhiFA_3(\cC_R)\bigr)
 \;\xrightarrow{\sim}\;
 B^{E_3}_{\MO}\bigl(\PhiFA_3(\cC_R)\bigr),
\]
with the top and bottom composites quasi-isomorphic up to a chain
homotopy whose class in $H^2(\Mbar_{2, 0}; \Q) = \Q \langle
\lambda_1 \rangle$ vanishes on $\lambda_1$ (Mumford boundary collapse).
The three Koszul-dual images agree after localisation at the
$R$-indexed Heegner wall, recovering the same chain-level numeric
$c_R(0)/2$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The Hodge-to-BV leg is the holomorphic twist of the topological $E_3$-
algebra (Costello--Li $2016$ Proposition $5.2$): the tree-level Kapranov
$L_\infty$ structure on $T_{X_R}[-1]$ and the Costello--Li one-loop BV
partition function share the same Koszul-dual source via the
Kontsevich--Tamarkin formality zig-zag. The BV-to-MO leg is the
Costello--Gaiotto--Yagi $2019$ 5D holomorphic Chern--Simons to Yangian
VOA equivalence: for simply-laced $\fg$, the perturbative $\hCS$
expansion converges to the Yangian VOA whose $R$-matrix is the
Maulik--Okounkov stable-envelope object. The Hodge-to-MO leg is the
Kapustin--Witten $2006$ geometric Satake--Witten duality refined by
Maulik--Okounkov $2014$ Theorem~$1.5$: the Satake equivalence identifies
the Kapranov $L_\infty$-twist with the MO stable envelope after
localisation to the equivariant chamber.

The triangle commutes up to the chain homotopy whose cohomology class
lives in $H^2(\Mbar_{2, 0}; \Q) = \Q\langle \lambda_1 \rangle$ (the
rational cohomology of $\Mbar_{2, 0}$ in degree $2$ is one-dimensional,
spanned by the first Hodge class). The cocycle restriction to
$\lambda_1$ vanishes by the Mumford boundary relation
$\partial^* \lambda_1^{\det}(g) =
\pi_1^* \lambda_1^{\det}(g_1) + \pi_2^*
\lambda_1^{\det}(g_2)$: the $\kch$-curving on $\PhiFA_3(\cC_R)$ coincides
with the Hodge-boundary-descent datum, so the $E_2$-transverse
correction to the $\lambda_1$-pairing cancels against the $\kch$-curving
at the boundary of $\Mbar_2$. Hence the three-path
quasi-isomorphism is fixed after choosing the common Drinfeld-associator
torsor point and the Hodge-boundary curving, and its cohomology class is
independent of the representative by the preceding $\GRTone(\Q)$
invariance.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Universality across $d$]
\label{rem:universality-across-d-master}
The master identity sits at $d = 3$; a $d = 5$ sibling replaces
$\mathrm{K3} \times E$ by $\mathrm{K3} \times \mathrm{K3} \times E$
and the Gritsenko paramodular cusp form $\Delta_5$ by Borcherds'
Fake Monster lift $\Phi_{12}$ on $\mathrm{II}_{25, 1}$ (Borcherds
$1990$, \emph{Adv. Math.}~$83$). The modular-operadic framework
generalises via the Getzler--Kapranov $E_d$-modular operad and the
Dunn--Lurie decomposition $E_5 \simeq E_2 \otimes E_2 \otimes E_1$
(see the $d$-stratified siblings discussion in
\cite{VolI}). The $N = 1$ analogue
at $d = 5$ is $\kBKM(\Phi_{12}) = c_1^{(d = 5)}(0)/2 = 12$ (Fake
Monster Borcherds lift weight $12$, Borcherds $1992$); the
chain-level modular-operadic realisation is the direct generalisation
to $\Mbar_{5, 0}$ modulo the Schottky obstruction that the programme
currently records as conjectural at $g \geq 4$.
\end{remark}
